# 📚 Pico 2500: Documentation (Projects 1001-1100)

---

## Batch 101: Advanced LCD Display (I2C) 2 (6-8 (Middle))

## 1. Project 1001: Advanced LCD Display (I2C) Sensor Setup

### 2. Learning Objective
"Bus Speed". Initialize I2C at 400kHz (Fast Mode) instead of 100kHz. Measure if `print` time decreases.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **I2C Baudrate**: Speed of communication clock (SCL). Standard=100k, Fast=400k, Fast+=1M.
*   **Optimization**: Reducing latency.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **I2C LCD**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LCD SDA** | GP0 | I2C0 Data |
| **LCD SCL** | GP1 | I2C0 Clock |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Communication**, drag **`init_i2c`**
*   from **Smart IO**, drag **`ticks_us`**, **`ticks_diff`**

### 7. Variables
*   **start, end, duration**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1.  **Initialize I2C Bus**:
    *   From the **Communication** category in the block palette, drag the **`init_i2c`** block into your workspace.
    *   Click on the block to reveal its configuration parameters.
    *   In the **SDA Pin** dropdown field, select **GP0** (this matches the wiring table above).
    *   In the **SCL Pin** dropdown field, select **GP1**.
    *   In the **Frequency** number input field, enter **400000** (this sets 400kHz Fast Mode instead of default 100kHz).
    *   From **Variables**, drag a **`set variable to`** block.
    *   In the variable name field, type **i2c**.
    *   Connect the `init_i2c` block output to this variable assignment.
2.  **Initialize LCD Display**:
    *   From **Smart Display** category, drag **`create_lcd`** block (or use direct Python initialization).
    *   In the **I2C Object** parameter, select the variable **i2c** from the dropdown.
    *   In the **I2C Address** hex input field, enter **0x27** (standard LCD I2C address).
    *   In the **Rows** number field, enter **2**.
    *   In the **Columns** number field, enter **16**.
    *   From **Variables**, drag a **`set variable to`** block.
    *   In the variable name field, type **lcd**.
    *   Connect the LCD creation block output to this variable.
3.  **Start Performance Timer**:
    *   From the **Smart IO** category, drag the **`ticks_us`** block.
    *   This block has no parameters (it just returns current microseconds).
    *   From **Variables**, drag a **`set variable to`** block.
    *   In the variable name field, type **start**.
    *   Connect the `ticks_us` block to this variable assignment.
4.  **Write Test String to Display**:
    *   From **Smart Display** category, drag the **`lcd_putstr`** block.
    *   In the **LCD Object** parameter dropdown, select variable **lcd**.
    *   In the **Text** string input field, enter **"Speed Test 400k"**.
    *   This writes text to the LCD, testing the I2C throughput at 400kHz.
    *   Place this block in sequence after the timer start.
5.  **End Performance Timer**:
    *   From the **Smart IO** category, drag another **`ticks_us`** block (same as step 3).
    *   From **Variables**, drag a **`set variable to`** block.
    *   In the variable name field, type **end**.
    *   Connect this second `ticks_us` block to capture the end time.
6.  **Calculate and Display Duration**:
    *   From the **Smart IO** category, drag the **`ticks_diff`** block.
    *   In the **End Time** parameter, select variable **end**.
    *   In the **Start Time** parameter, select variable **start**.
    *   This calculates the microseconds elapsed.
    *   From **Text**, drag a **`print`** block.
    *   From **Text**, drag a **`join`** or format block to create message.
    *   Format the output as: "Time: [duration] us".
    *   Note: At 400kHz, expect ~15ms. At 100kHz, expect ~45ms (3x slower).

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Run**: "Time: 15ms" (vs 45ms at 100k).
2.  **Result**: Faster updates = smoother animation.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime
from pico_i2c_lcd import I2cLcd

i2c = machine.I2C(0, sda=machine.Pin(0), scl=machine.Pin(1), freq=400000)
lcd = I2cLcd(i2c, 0x27, 2, 16)

start = utime.ticks_us()
lcd.putstr("Speed Test 400k")
end = utime.ticks_us()

print(f"Time: {utime.ticks_diff(end, start)} us")
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Cabling**: High speed requires better wires/connections. Long wires might fail at 400k.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **1MHz**: Try `freq=1000000`. Does the LCD controller support it? (Usually not).

## 1. Project 1002: Advanced LCD Display (I2C) Data Reader

### 2. Learning Objective
"Hex Dump". Read memory address `0x20000000` (RAM). Display the first 8 bytes in Hex format on LCD.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Memory Map**: Accessing raw RAM.
*   **Hexadecimal**: Base-16 representation `0xAF`.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **LCD**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LCD SDA** | GP0 | I2C0 |
| **LCD SCL** | GP1 | I2C0 |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Text**, drag **`to_hex`**
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set_variable`**

### 7. Variables
*   **byte_val**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1.  **Set Memory Address**:
    *   From **Variables**, drag a **`set variable to`** block.
    *   In the variable name field, type **addr**.
    *   From **Math**, drag a **`number`** block.
    *   In the number block, enter **0x20000000** (hexadecimal format for RAM start).
    *   This is the beginning of SRAM, which is safe to read from.
2.  **Create Empty List for Bytes**:
    *   From **Lists**, drag a **`create empty list`** block.
    *   From **Variables**, drag **`set variable to`** block.
    *   Name the variable **bytes_list**.
    *   Connect the empty list to this variable.
3.  **Read 8 Bytes from Memory** (Loop):
    *   From **Loops**, drag a **`repeat`** block.
    *   In the **times** field, enter **8** (to read 8 bytes).
    *   From **Loops**, drag a **`counter variable`** block and set it to **i** (0-7).
    *   Inside the loop:
        - From **Advanced/Python**, drag **`machine.mem8[]`** block.
        - In the bracket, use **Math** → **`add`** block.
        - Add **addr** variable + **i** counter.
        - This reads byte at address `0x20000000 + i`.
    *   From **Lists**, drag **`add item to list`** block.
    *   Add the read byte value to **bytes_list**.
4.  **Convert Each Byte to Hex String**:
    *   From **Lists**, drag a **`for each item`** loop.
    *   Set to loop through **bytes_list**.
    *   Inside this loop:
        - From **Text**, drag **`format`** block or Python f-string block.
        - Use format **"{:02X}"** to convert byte to 2-digit hex.
        - The **:02X** means: 0-padded, 2 digits, uppercase hex.
    *   From **Lists**, drag **`join list with separator`** block.
    *   In the **separator** field, enter a **space** character.
    *   This creates: "2F A0 B1 C3 D4 E5 F6 07".
5.  **Display on LCD**:
    *   From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_clear`** block.
    *   Select **lcd** variable as the object.
    *   From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_move_to`** block.
    *   Set **column** to **0**, **row** to **0**.
    *   From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_putstr`** block.
    *   In the **text** field, use **Text** → **`join`** block.
    *   Combine literal text **"Mem:"** with the hex string.
    *   Truncate to 13 characters (to fit "Mem:" + 13 chars on 16-char display).
    *   Example output shown: **"Mem:2F A0 B1 C3"** (first 4 bytes visible).

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Boot**: LCD shows raw memory "garbage" (data).
2.  **Reset**: Might change.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
from pico_i2c_lcd import I2cLcd

i2c = machine.I2C(0, sda=machine.Pin(0), scl=machine.Pin(1), freq=400000)
lcd = I2cLcd(i2c, 0x27, 2, 16)

addr = 0x20000000  # Start of RAM
bytes_list = []

# Read 8 bytes
for i in range(8):
    byte_val = machine.mem8[addr + i]
    bytes_list.append(f"{byte_val:02X}")  # Format as 2-digit hex

# Create display string
hex_str = " ".join(bytes_list)
lcd.clear()
lcd.putstr(f"Mem:{hex_str[:13]}")  # Fit on 16-char display
print(f"Memory at 0x20000000: {hex_str}")
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **SegFault**: Reading restricted memory areas causes Hard Fault (crash).

### 12. Try This Next
*   **View Stack**: Read stack pointer address.

## 1. Project 1003: Advanced LCD Display (I2C) Threshold Logic

### 2. Learning Objective
"Auto-Scroll". Write function `print_long(text)`. If text > 16 chars, automatically scroll it. If < 16, center it.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **String Manipulation**: Length check `len()`.
*   **Animation Loop**: Scrolling text.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **LCD**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LCD SDA** | GP0 | I2C0 |
| **LCD SCL** | GP1 | I2C0 |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Logic**, drag **`if_else`**
*   From **Text**, drag **`length`**

### 7. Variables
*   **txt**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1.  **Create Function Definition**:
    *   From **Functions**, drag **`define function`** block.
    *   In the function name field, enter **print_long**.
    *   Click **Add Parameter** button.
    *   Name the parameter **text** (this will be the string to display).
    *   All subsequent blocks go inside this function.
2.  **Get Text Length**:
    *   From **Text**, drag the **`length of`** block.
    *   In the text input, select the **text** parameter (from function input).
    *   From **Variables**, drag **`set variable to`** block.
    *   Name it **text_len**.
    *   Connect the length block output to this variable.
3.  **Check If Text Fits on Display**:
    *   From **Logic**, drag an **`if_else`** block.
    *   From **Logic**, drag a **`comparison`** block (< > =).
    *   Set it to: **text_len** **≤** **16** (less than or equal to LCD width).
4.  **Branch A: Handle Short Text (Centering)**:
    *   In the **if** section (when text ≤ 16 chars):
        - From **Math**, drag **`subtract`** block: **16 - text_len**.
        - From **Math**, drag **`divide`** block: divide result by **2**.
        - From **Math**, drag **`floor`** or **`integer divide //`** to round down.
        - From **Variables**, set this to variable **pad**.
    *   From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_move_to`** block.
    *   Set **column** parameter to variable **pad**.
    *   Set **row** parameter to **0**.
    *   From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_putstr`** block.
    *   In **text** parameter, select the function parameter **text**.
    *   This centers the text on the display.
5.  **Branch B: Handle Long Text (Scrolling)**:
    *   In the **else** section (when text > 16 chars):
        - From **Math**, drag **`subtract`** block: **text_len - 16**.
        - This gives the maximum scroll position.
    *   From **Loops**, drag a **`for`** loop.
    *   Set counter variable to **i**.
    *   Set range from **0** to **(text_len - 16)**.
    *   Inside the loop:
        - From **Text**, drag **`substring`** or **`slice`** block.
        - Set **start** to variable **i**.
        - Set **end** to **i + 16**.
        - This extracts a 16-character window.
        - From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_move_to`** block (column **0**, row **0**).
        - From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_putstr`** block with the substring.
        - from **Smart IO**, drag **`sleep`** block.
        - Set sleep duration to **0.3** seconds (pause so user can read).
6.  **Test the Function**:
    *   Outside the function, from **Functions**, drag **`call print_long`** block.
    *   In the **text** parameter, enter test string **"Hello"**.
    *   Observe: text appears centered as **"     Hello      "**.
    *   Try again with **"Welcome to the Pico World"**.
    *   Observe: text scrolls across the display smoothly.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Short**: "Hello" -> "     Hello      ".
2.  **Long**: "Welcome to the Pico World" -> Scrolls across.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import utime
from pico_i2c_lcd import I2cLcd
import machine

i2c = machine.I2C(0, sda=machine.Pin(0), scl=machine.Pin(1), freq=400000)
lcd = I2cLcd(i2c, 0x27, 2, 16)

def print_long(text):  # Correct function name
    lcd.clear()
    if len(text) <= 16:
        # Center short text
        pad = (16 - len(text)) // 2
        lcd.move_to(pad, 0)
        lcd.putstr(text)
    else:
        # Scroll long text
        for i in range(len(text) - 15):
            lcd.move_to(0, 0)
            lcd.putstr(text[i:i+16])
            utime.sleep(0.3)

# Test the function
print_long("Hello")  # Should center
utime.sleep(2)
print_long("Welcome to the Pico World")  # Should scroll
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Blocking**: The scroll loop blocks other code.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Non-Blocking**: Use a timer/state machine to scroll line 1 while updating line 2.

## 1. Project 1004: Advanced LCD Display (I2C) Status Monitor

### 2. Learning Objective
"System Monitor". Screen 1: "CPU Freq". Screen 2: "Free RAM". Screen 3: "Uptime". Cycle every 3s.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **System Stats**: `gc.mem_free()`, `machine.freq()`.
*   **Carousel UI**: Cycling pages.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **LCD**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LCD SDA** | GP0 | I2C0 |
| **LCD SCL** | GP1 | I2C0 |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   from **Smart IO**, drag **`sleep`**, **`ticks_ms`**
*   From **System & Storage**, drag **`machine_freq`**, **`gc_mem_free`**, **`gc_collect`**
*   From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_clear`**, **`lcd_putstr`**
*   From **Text**, drag **`format`** or **`join`**
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set_variable`**

### 7. Variables
*   **page**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1.  **Initialize Page Counter**:
    *   From **Variables**, drag a **`set variable to`** block.
    *   In the variable name field, type **page**.
    *   From **Math**, drag a **`number`** block and set it to **0**.
    *   This tracks which screen to display (0=CPU, 1=RAM, 2=Uptime).
2.  **Create Main Loop**:
    *   From **Loops**, drag a **`while True`** block.
    *   All the following steps go inside this infinite loop.
3.  **Display CPU Frequency (Page 0)**:
    *   From **Logic**, drag an **`if_elseif_else`** block.
    *   From **Logic**, drag a **`comparison`** block (=).
    *   Set condition: **page** **==** **0**.
    *   Inside this if section:
        - From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_clear`** block to clear the screen.
        - From **System & Storage**, drag **`machine_freq`** block (no parameters - returns CPU frequency in Hz).
        - From **Text**, drag **`join`** block or **`format`** block.
        - Create text: **"CPU: "** + **freq value**.
        - From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_putstr`** block.
        - In the **text** parameter, use the joined string.
        - Result displays: "CPU: 125000000" (125 MHz).
4.  **Display Free RAM (Page 1)**:
    *   In the **elseif** section:
    *   Set condition: **page** **==** **1**.
    *   Inside this section:
        - From **System & Storage**, drag **`gc_collect`** block first (optional - forces garbage collection).
        - From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_clear`** block.
        - From **System & Storage**, drag **`gc_mem_free`** block (returns free RAM in bytes).
        - From **Text**, drag **`join`** or **`format`** block.
        - Create text: **"Free: "** + **memory value**.
        - From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_putstr`** block with this text.
        - Result displays: "Free: 150000" (150 KB free).
5.  **Display Uptime (Page 2)**:
    *   In the **else** section (page == 2):
        - From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_clear`** block.
        - from **Smart IO**, drag **`ticks_ms`** block (time since boot in milliseconds).
        - From **Math**, drag **`divide`** block: divide by **1000** to get seconds.
        - From **Text**, drag **`join`** or **`format`** block.
        - Create text: **"Time: "** + **seconds** + **"s"**.
        - From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_putstr`** block.
        - Result displays: "Time: 12s".
6.  **Wait and Cycle to Next Page**:
    *   After the if/elseif/else block (still in while loop):
    *   from **Smart IO**, drag **`sleep`** block.
    *   Set duration to **3** seconds (gives user time to read).
    *   From **Math**, drag **`add`** block: **page + 1**.
    *   From **Math**, drag **`modulo`** block: result **%** **3** (wraps 0→1→2→0).
    *   From **Variables**, drag **`set variable`** block.
    *   Assign the modulo result back to variable **page**.
    *   This automatically cycles through screens.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **LCD**: "CPU: 125000000" ... "RAM: 150000" ... "Time: 12s".

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime
import gc
from pico_i2c_lcd import I2cLcd

i2c = machine.I2C(0, sda=machine.Pin(0), scl=machine.Pin(1), freq=400000)
lcd = I2cLcd(i2c, 0x27, 2, 16)

page = 0  # Track current screen
start_time = utime.ticks_ms()  # For uptime calculation

while True:
    lcd.clear()
    
    if page == 0:
        # Screen 1: CPU Frequency
        freq = machine.freq()
        lcd.putstr(f"CPU: {freq}")
        
    elif page == 1:
        # Screen 2: Free RAM
        gc.collect()  # Optional: clean up first
        free_mem = gc.mem_free()
        lcd.putstr(f"Free: {free_mem}")
        
    else:  # page == 2
        # Screen 3: Uptime
        uptime_ms = utime.ticks_diff(utime.ticks_ms(), start_time)
        uptime_sec = uptime_ms // 1000
        lcd.putstr(f"Time: {uptime_sec}s")
    
    # Wait 3 seconds
    utime.sleep(3)
    
    # Cycle to next page (0 -> 1 -> 2 -> 0)
    page = (page + 1) % 3
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Garbage**: Not running `gc.collect()` might show fragmentation.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Button**: Switch page on button click instead of timer.

---

## 1. Project 1005: Automated Advanced LCD Display (I2C) Controller

### 2. Learning Objective
"Sprite Anim". Define custom chars for "Pacman Open" and "Pacman Closed". Animate him eating dots across screen.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Custom Characters**: Defining usually 5x8 bitmaps. 8 bytes per char.
*   **Frame Animation**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **LCD**

### 5. Wiring /Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LCD SDA** | GP0 | I2C0 |
| **LCD SCL** | GP1 | I2C0 |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Data**, drag **`create_bytearray`** or use Python code
*   From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_custom_char`**, **`lcd_move_to`**, **`lcd_putchar`**
*   From **Loops**, drag **`for_range`**, **`repeat`**
*   from **Smart IO**, drag **`sleep`**
*   From **Math**, drag **`modulo`**

### 7. Variables
*   **pac_open, pac_closed**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1.  **Create Pacman Open Mouth Character Data**:
    *   From **Data** or **Python**, create a **`bytearray`** with 8 bytes.
    *   Each byte represents one row of the 5x8 pixel custom character.
    *   For "mouth open" pattern, use values: **[0x0E, 0x1D, 0x1F, 0x1C, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x0E, 0x00]**.
    *   From **Variables**, drag **`set variable to`** block.
    *   Name it **pac_open**.
    *   Assign the bytearray to this variable.
2.  **Create Pacman Closed Mouth Character Data**:
    *   Similarly, create another **`bytearray`** for closed mouth.
    *   Use values: **[0x0E, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x0E, 0x00]** (full circle).
    *   From **Variables**, drag **`set variable to`** block.
    *   Name it **pac_closed**.
3.  **Load Custom Characters into LCD Memory**:
    *   From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_custom_char`** block.
    *   In the **slot** parameter, enter **0** (custom char slot 0).
    *   In the **data** parameter, select variable **pac_open**.
    *   This stores the open mouth pattern in slot 0.
    *   Drag another **`lcd_custom_char`** block.
    *   Set **slot** to **1**.
    *   Set **data** to variable **pac_closed**.
4.  **Animate Pacman Across Screen**:
    *   From **Loops**, drag a **`for`** loop.
    *   Set counter variable to **i**.
    *   Set range from **0** to **15** (16 positions across LCD).
    *   Inside the loop:
        - From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_move_to`** block.
        - Set **column** parameter to variable **i** (current position).
        - Set **row** parameter to **0**.
        - From **Math**, drag **`modulo`** block: **i % 2** (alternates 0, 1, 0, 1...).
        - From **Text**, drag **`chr`** block to convert number to character.
        - From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_putchar`** block.
        - Pass the chr(i%2) to display alternating pac_open/pac_closed.
        - from **Smart IO**, drag **`sleep`** block.
        - Set duration to **0.2** seconds (creates "waka waka" animation).
5.  **Observe Animation**:
    *   Run the program.
    *   Watch Pacman "eat" across the screen with mouth opening/closing.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **LCD**: Pacman "waka waka" moves right.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
# standard 5x8 pixel bits
pac_o = bytearray([0x0E,0x1D,0x1F,0x1C,0x1F,0x1F,0x0E,0x00])
pac_c = bytearray([0x0E,0x1F,0x1F,0x1F,0x1F,0x1F,0x0E,0x00])

lcd.custom_char(0, pac_o)
lcd.custom_char(1, pac_c)

for i in range(16):
    lcd.move_to(i, 0)
    lcd.putchar(chr(i%2)) # Toggle 0/1
    utime.sleep(0.2)
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Address**: Only 0-7 custom slots usually available.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Ghost**: Define a Ghost character to chase him.

---

## 1. Project 1006: Advanced LCD Display (I2C) Safety Trigger

### 2. Learning Objective
"Visual Alarm". Invert the display colors (if supported by controller commands) or flash Backlight continuously if `Temperature > Critical`.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Visual Alerting**.
*   **Backlight Control**: Often controlled via I2C byte or separate pin.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **LCD**
*   **Temp Sensor**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LCD SDA** | GP0 | I2C0 |
| **LCD SCL** | GP1 | I2C0 |
| **Temp Sensor** | GP26 | ADC or Digital |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Sensing**, drag **`read_adc`** or **`read_temperature`** (depending on sensor type)
*   From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_backlight_on`**, **`lcd_backlight_off`**
*   From **Logic**, drag **`if_else`**, **`comparison`**
*   from **Smart IO**, drag **`sleep`**
*   From **Loops**, drag **`while_true`**

### 7. Variables
*   **t** (temperature reading)
*   **threshold** (critical temperature value)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1.  **Define Critical Temperature Threshold**:
    *   From **Variables**, drag a **`set variable to`** block.
    *   In the variable name field, type **threshold**.
    *   From **Math**, drag a **`number`** block.
    *   Set value to **30** (degrees Celsius as critical temperature).
2.  **Create Main Loop**:
    *   From **Loops**, drag a **`while True`** block.
    *   All monitoring code goes inside this infinite loop.
3.  **Read Temperature Sensor**:
    *   From **Sensing**, drag **`read_adc`** block (if using analog temp sensor).
    *   In the **Pin** parameter, select **GP26** (matches wiring table).
    *   OR from **Sensing**, drag **`read_temperature`** (if using digital sensor like DHT11).
    *   From **Variables**, drag **`set variable to`** block.
    *   Name it **t** (for temperature).
    *   Connect the sensor reading to this variable.
4.  **Check Temperature Against Threshold**:
    *   From **Logic**, drag an **`if_else`** block.
    *   From **Logic**, drag a **`comparison`** block (>).
    *   Set condition: **t** **>** **threshold** (e.g., temp > 30°C).
5.  **Flash Backlight When Critical (If Branch)**:
    *   Inside the **if** section (when temperature is too high):
        - From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_backlight_off`** block.
        - In the **LCD object** parameter, select your LCD variable.
        - This turns backlight OFF.
        - from **Smart IO**, drag **`sleep`** block.
        - Set duration to **0.2** seconds.
        - From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_backlight_on`** block.
        - from **Smart IO**, drag **`sleep`** block again.
        - Set duration to **0.2** seconds.
        - This creates a visible flashing pattern (200ms on, 200ms off).
6.  **Keep Backlight Steady When Normal (Else Branch)**:
    *   Inside the **else** section (temperature is safe):
        - From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_backlight_on`** block.
        - Ensures backlight stays on during normal operation.
7.  **Add Small Delay Before Next Check**:
    *   After the if/else block (still in while loop):
    *   from **Smart IO**, drag **`sleep`** block.
    *   Set duration to **0.1** seconds.
    *   Prevents excessive CPU usage.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Safe**: Steady light.
2.  **Hot**: Screen flashes annoying blink pattern.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime
from pico_i2c_lcd import I2cLcd

# Initialize I2C and LCD
i2c = machine.I2C(0, sda=machine.Pin(0), scl=machine.Pin(1), freq=400000)
lcd = I2cLcd(i2c, 0x27, 2, 16)

# Initialize temperature sensor (example: ADC-based)
temp_adc = machine.ADC(26)  # GP26

def read_temp():
    # Convert ADC reading to temperature
    # This is a simplified example - adjust for your sensor
    adc_value = temp_adc.read_u16()
    voltage = adc_value * (3.3 / 65535)
    # Example: TMP36 sensor formula
    temp_c = (voltage - 0.5) * 100
    return temp_c

threshold = 30  # Critical temperature in Celsius

while True:
    t = read_temp()
    
    if t > threshold:
        # Flash backlight for alarm
        lcd.backlight_off()
        utime.sleep(0.2)
        lcd.backlight_on()
        utime.sleep(0.2)
    else:
        # Keep backlight on normally
        lcd.backlight_on()
    
    utime.sleep(0.1)  # Small delay
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Library**: Some simple libs don't expose backlight control easily.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Message**: Flash "WARNING" text too.

## 1. Project 1007: Advanced LCD Display (I2C) Data Logger

### 2. Learning Objective
"Log Viewer". Read `log.txt` from Flash. Display last 3 lines on screen (using Up/Down buttons to scroll file).

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **File I/O**: Reading lines into a list.
*   **UI List View**: Scrolling through a collection.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **LCD**
*   **2 Buttons**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LCD SDA** | GP0 | I2C0 |
| **LCD SCL** | GP1 | I2C0 |
| **BTN Up** | GP15 | Scroll Up |
| **BTN Down** | GP14 | Scroll Down |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **System & Storage**, drag **`open`**, **`read`**, **`readlines`**
*   From **Lists**, drag **`get_item`**, **`length`**
*   From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_clear`**, **`lcd_putstr`**, **`lcd_move_to`**
*   From **Inputs**, drag **`digital_read`**
*   From **Logic**, drag **`if`**, **`and`**
*   From **Math**, drag **`subtract`**, **`add`**
*   From **Loops**, drag **`while_true`**
*   from **Smart IO**, drag **`sleep`**

### 7. Variables
*   **lines** (list of log file lines)
*   **idx** (current index for viewing)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1.  **Read Log File from Flash**:
    *   From **System & Storage**, drag **`open`** block.
    *   In the **filename** parameter, enter **"log.txt"**.
    *   In the **mode** parameter, enter **"r"** (read mode).
    *   From **System & Storage**, drag **`readlines`** method.
    *   This reads all lines into a list.
    *   From **Variables**, drag **`set variable to`** block.
    *   Name it **lines**.
    *   Connect the readlines output to this variable.
2.  **Initialize Index Counter**:
    *   From **Variables**, drag **`set variable to`** block.
    *   Name it **idx**.
    *   Set initial value to **0** (start at first line).
3.  **Initialize Buttons with Pull-ups**:
    *   From **Inputs**, drag **`init_button`** block.
    *   For Up button: Set pin to **GP15**, enable internal **pull-up**.
    *   For Down button: Set pin to **GP14**, enable internal **pull-up**.
    *   Buttons read **0** when pressed (active low).
4.  **Create Main Display Loop**:
    *   From **Loops**, drag **`while True`** block.
    *   All display and navigation logic goes inside.
5.  **Clear and Display Current Lines**:
    *   From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_clear`** block to erase screen.
    *   From **Lists**, drag **`get_item`** block.
    *   Get item at index **idx** from **lines** list.
    *   From **Text**, drag **`substring`** block to limit to 16 characters.
    *   From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_putstr`** to show on row 0.
    *   From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_move_to`** block.
    *   Set position to **column 0**, **row 1**.
    *   Get item at **idx + 1** (next line).
    *   Display on row 1 (if it exists).
6.  **Handle Down Button (Scroll Down)**:
    *   From **Inputs**, drag **`digital_read`** for button Down (GP14).
    *   From **Logic**, drag **`if`** block.
    *   Condition: button value **== 0** (pressed) **AND** **idx < len(lines) - 2**.
    *   Inside if:
        - From **Math**, drag **`add`** block: **idx = idx + 1**.
        - from **Smart IO**, drag **`sleep`** block, set to **0.2** seconds (debounce).
7.  **Handle Up Button (Scroll Up)**:
    *   From **Inputs**, drag **`digital_read`** for button Up (GP15).
    *   From **Logic**, drag **`if`** block.
    *   Condition: button value **== 0** (pressed) **AND** **idx > 0**.
    *   Inside if:
        - From **Math**, drag **`subtract`** block: **idx = idx - 1**.
        - from **Smart IO**, drag **`sleep`** block, set to **0.2** seconds (debounce).
8.  **Small Loop Delay**:
    *   from **Smart IO**, drag **`sleep`** block.
    *   Set to **0.05** seconds to prevent excessive CPU usage.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Click-Click**: Browsing history of logs.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime
from pico_i2c_lcd import I2cLcd

# Initialize I2C and LCD
i2c = machine.I2C(0, sda=machine.Pin(0), scl=machine.Pin(1), freq=400000)
lcd = I2cLcd(i2c, 0x27, 2, 16)

# Initialize buttons with pull-up resistors
btn_up = machine.Pin(15, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_UP)
btn_down = machine.Pin(14, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_UP)

# Read log file
try:
    with open('log.txt', 'r') as f:
        lines = f.readlines()
except OSError:
    lines = ["No log file", "found"]

# Remove trailing newlines
lines = [line.strip() for line in lines]

idx = 0  # Current viewing index

while True:
    lcd.clear()
    
    # Display current line (row 0)
    if idx < len(lines):
        lcd.putstr(lines[idx][:16])  # Truncate to 16 chars
    
    # Display next line (row 1)
    lcd.move_to(0, 1)
    if idx + 1 < len(lines):
        lcd.putstr(lines[idx + 1][:16])
    
    # Check Down button (scroll forward)
    if btn_down.value() == 0 and idx < len(lines) - 2:
        idx += 1
        utime.sleep(0.2)  # Debounce
    
    # Check Up button (scroll backward)
    if btn_up.value() == 0 and idx > 0:
        idx -= 1
        utime.sleep(0.2)  # Debounce
    
    utime.sleep(0.05)  # Small delay
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Memory**: Reading a HUGE log file into RAM crashes Pico. Read line by line or use offsets for big files.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Tail**: Just confirm "Last 3 lines". `f.seek()` to end?

## 1. Project 1008: Smart Advanced LCD Display (I2C) System

### 2. Learning Objective
"Chat Client". Rx UART. Display incoming message on Row 1. Typer your reply (Rotary Encoder select char) on Row 2.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Full Duplex UI**: Showing RX while inputting TX.
*   **Text Entry**: Choosing letters with a knob.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **LCD**
*   **Rotary Encoder**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LCD SDA** | GP4 | I2C0 Data |
| **LCD SCL** | GP5 | I2C0 Clock |
| **UART TX** | GP0 | Serial transmit |
| **UART RX** | GP1 | Serial receive |
| **Encoder A** | GP16 | Rotation input |
| **Encoder B** | GP17 | Rotation input |
| **Encoder BTN** | GP18 | Push button |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Communication**, drag **`uart_init`**, **`uart_read`**, **`uart_write`**, **`uart_any`**
*   From **Inputs**, drag **`rotary_encoder_init`**, **`encoder_read`**, **`digital_read`**
*   From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_clear`**, **`lcd_move_to`**, **`lcd_putstr`**
*   From **Text**, drag **`chr`**, **`ord`**, **`join`**
*   From **Logic**, drag **`if`**
*   From **Math**, drag **`add`**, **`modulo`**
*   From **Loops**, drag **`while_true`**

### 7. Variables
*   **rx_msg** (received UART message)
*   **tx_buf** (transmit buffer being composed)
*   **current_char_code** (ASCII code for current character selection)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1.  **Initialize UART Communication**:
    *   From **Communication**, drag **`uart_init`** block.
    *   Set **UART ID** to **0**.
    *   Set **TX pin** to **GP0**.
    *   Set **RX pin** to **GP1**.
    *   Set **baud rate** to **9600** (standard speed).
2.  **Initialize I2C and LCD** (different pins to avoid conflict):
    *   From **Communication**, drag **`init_i2c`** block.
    *   Set **SDA** to **GP4**, **SCL** to **GP5**.
    *   Initialize LCD on this I2C bus.
3.  **Initialize Rotary Encoder**:
    *   From **Inputs**, drag **`rotary_encoder_init`** block.
    *   Set **A pin** to **GP16**, **B pin** to **GP17**.
    *   Set **button pin** to **GP18** with pull-up enabled.
4.  **Initialize Variables**:
    *   Set **current_char_code** to **65** (ASCII 'A').
    *   Set **tx_buf** to empty string **""**.
    *   Set **rx_msg** to empty string.
5.  **Create Main Loop**:
    *   From **Loops**, drag **`while True`** block.
6.  **Check for Incoming UART Data** (Non-blocking):
    *   From **Communication**, drag **`uart_any`** block.
    *   From **Logic**, drag **`if`** block with condition: UART has data.
    *   Inside if:
        - From **Communication**, drag **`uart_read`** block.
        - Decode bytes to string.
        - Store in **rx_msg** variable.
        - From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_move_to`** (row 0).
        - Display **rx_msg** truncated to 16 characters.
7.  **Read Rotary Encoder for Character Selection**:
    *   From **Inputs**, drag **`encoder_read`** block.
    *   Get rotation delta (positive = clockwise, negative = counter-clockwise).
    *   From **Math**, drag **`add`**: **current_char_code += delta**.
    *   From **Math**, drag **`modulo`** to wrap: keep between 32-126 (printable ASCII).
8.  **Display Current Composition on Row 2**:
    *   From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_move_to`** (column 0, row 1).
    *   From **Text**, drag **`chr`** to convert **current_char_code** to character.
    *   From **Text**, drag **`join`**: combine **tx_buf + current character**.
    *   Display result (truncated to 16 chars).
9.  **Check Encoder Button Press** (Add Character):
    *   From **Inputs**, drag **`digital_read`** on GP18.
    *   If button pressed (value == 0):
        - Append current character to **tx_buf**.
        - Add small delay for debounce (0.2s).
10. **Send Message** (Double-click Detection or separate send button):
    *   Simplified: When tx_buf reaches certain length or special character.
    *   From **Communication**, drag **`uart_write`** block.
    *   Send **tx_buf** over UART.
    *   Clear **tx_buf** after sending.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Type**: Spin spin click 'H'... Spin click 'i'.
2.  **Send**: Sends "Hi" over UART.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime
from pico_i2c_lcd import I2cLcd

# Initialize UART (GP0 = TX, GP1 = RX)
uart = machine.UART(0, baudrate=9600, tx=machine.Pin(0), rx=machine.Pin(1))

# Initialize I2C and LCD (GP4 = SDA, GP5 = SCL to avoid conflict)
i2c = machine.I2C(0, sda=machine.Pin(4), scl=machine.Pin(5), freq=400000)
lcd = I2cLcd(i2c, 0x27, 2, 16)

# Initialize rotary encoder
enc_a = machine.Pin(16, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_UP)
enc_b = machine.Pin(17, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_UP)
enc_btn = machine.Pin(18, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_UP)

# Simple encoder state tracking
enc_last = enc_a.value()
current_char_code = 65  # Start at 'A'
tx_buf = ""
rx_msg = ""

while True:
    # Check for incoming UART data
    if uart.any():
        data = uart.read()
        rx_msg = data.decode('utf-8', 'ignore')[:16]
        lcd.move_to(0, 0)
        lcd.putstr(rx_msg.ljust(16))  # Pad to clear old text
    
    # Read encoder rotation (simplified)
    enc_curr = enc_a.value()
    if enc_curr != enc_last:
        if enc_b.value() != enc_curr:
            current_char_code += 1  # Clockwise
        else:
            current_char_code -= 1  # Counter-clockwise
        
        # Keep in printable ASCII range (32-126)
        if current_char_code > 126:
            current_char_code = 32
        elif current_char_code < 32:
            current_char_code = 126
        
        enc_last = enc_curr
    
    # Display current composition on row 2
    char_display = chr(current_char_code)
    display_text = (tx_buf + char_display)[-16:]  # Last 16 chars
    lcd.move_to(0, 1)
    lcd.putstr(display_text.ljust(16))
    
    # Check encoder button (add character)
    if enc_btn.value() == 0:
        tx_buf += char_display
        utime.sleep(0.2)  # Debounce
        
        # Send if newline character selected
        if current_char_code == 10:  # Newline = send
            uart.write(tx_buf.encode())
            tx_buf = ""
    
    utime.sleep(0.01)
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **UX**: Typing with an encoder is slow.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **PS/2 Keyboard**: Use a real keyboard?

## 1. Project 1009: Advanced LCD Display (I2C) Calibration Tool

### 2. Learning Objective
"Display Test Pattern". Fill screen with `0xFF` (Block). Then Checkerboard. Help find dead pixels or poor contrast.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Diagnostics**.
*   **Contrast Potentiometer**: Physical adjustment interaction.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **LCD**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LCD SDA** | GP0 | I2C0 |
| **LCD SCL** | GP1 | I2C0 |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Loops**, drag **`repeat`**, **`for`**
*   From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_clear`**, **`lcd_putchar`**, **`lcd_putstr`**
*   From **Text**, drag **`chr`**
*   from **Smart IO**, drag **`sleep`**

### 7. Variables
*   **pattern**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1.  **Create Main Test Loop**:
    *   From **Loops**, drag **`while True`** block.
2.  **Display Full Block Pattern**:
    *   From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_clear`** to erase screen.
    *   From **Loops**, drag **`for`** loop.
    *   Set range from **0** to **31** (2 rows × 16 columns = 32 positions).
    *   Inside loop:
        - From **Text**, drag **`chr`** block.
        - Pass value **0xFF** or **255** (creates solid block character).
        - From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_putchar`** with this character.
    *   This fills entire display with solid blocks.
3.  **Wait for Visual Inspection**:
    *   from **Smart IO**, drag **`sleep`** block.
    *   Set duration to **2** seconds.
    *   User can observe block pattern and adjust contrast potentiometer.
4.  **Display Adjustment Prompt**:
    *   From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_clear`**.
    *   From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_putstr`**.
    *   Display text: **"Adjust Contrast"**.
    *   from **Smart IO**, drag **`sleep`** for **2** seconds.
5.  **Optional: Checkerboard Pattern**:
    *   Alternate between block (0xFF) and space (0x20) characters.
    *   Creates checkerboard to test individual pixel clarity.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Test**: Solid blocks.
2.  **User**: Turns screw on back of LCD until blocks are crisp black.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime
from pico_i2c_lcd import I2cLcd

# Initialize I2C and LCD
i2c = machine.I2C(0, sda=machine.Pin(0), scl=machine.Pin(1), freq=400000)
lcd = I2cLcd(i2c, 0x27, 2, 16)

while True:
    # Test Pattern 1: Solid blocks
    lcd.clear()
    for i in range(32):  # 2 rows × 16 cols = 32 positions
        lcd.putchar(chr(0xFF))  # Full block character
    utime.sleep(2)
    
    # Prompt for adjustment
    lcd.clear()
    lcd.putstr("Adjust Contrast")
    utime.sleep(2)
    
    # Test Pattern 2: Checkerboard (optional)
    lcd.clear()
    for i in range(32):
        if i % 2 == 0:
            lcd.putchar(chr(0xFF))  # Block
        else:
            lcd.putchar(' ')  # Space
    utime.sleep(2)
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Invisible**: If contrast is wrong, you see nothing. This test confirms code works, hardware needs adjustment.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Blink**: Toggle backlight.

---

## 1. Project 1010: Advanced Advanced LCD Display (I2C) Prototype

### 2. Learning Objective
"Big Digits". Use custom chars (segments) to draw numbers that span 2 rows high (Big Font). Display Clock.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Typography**: Creating fonts.
*   **Compositing**: building a '3' from 2 top blocks and 2 bottom blocks.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **LCD**
*   **RTC**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LCD SDA** | GP0 | I2C0 |
| **LCD SCL** | GP1 | I2C0 |
| **RTC SDA** | GP0 | Same I2C |
| **RTC SCL** | GP1 | Same I2C |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_custom_char`**, **`lcd_move_to`**, **`lcd_putchar`**
*   from **Smart IO**, drag **`rtc_get_time`**, **`sleep`**
*   From **Data**, drag **`create_bytearray`**, **`create_list`**
*   From **Functions**, drag **`define_function`**, **`call_function`**
*   From **Text**, drag **`chr`**, **`string_from_list`**
*   From **Loops**, drag **`while_true`**

### 7. Variables
*   **hours, minutes** (current time)
*   **digit_patterns** (lookup table for big digit segments)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1.  **Define Custom Character Segments**:
    *   Create 8 custom characters for digit segments:
        - **Char 0**: Top-left curve
        - **Char 1**: Top-right curve  
        - **Char 2**: Horizontal bar (top/middle)
        - **Char 3**: Bottom-left curve
        - **Char 4**: Bottom-right curve
        - **Char 5**: Vertical left bar
        - **Char 6**: Vertical right bar
        - **Char 7**: Full block
    *   From **Data**, create bytearrays for each (8 bytes per character).
    *   From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_custom_char`** for each.
    *   Load all 8 patterns into LCD memory (slots 0-7).
2.  **Create Digit Pattern Lookup Table**:
    *   From **Data**, drag **`create_list`**.
    *   Define how each digit (0-9) maps to segments:
        - Example: '0' = top row uses chars [0, 2, 1], bottom row uses [3, 7, 4]
        - '1' = top [7, 6], bottom [7, 6]
        - '2' = top [2, 2, 1], bottom [3, 2, 2]
    *   Store in variable **digit_patterns**.
3.  **Create Draw Big Digit Function**:
    *   From **Functions**, drag **`define_function`**.
    *   Name it **draw_big_digit**.
    *   Parameters: **digit** (0-9), **x_position** (column).
    *   Inside function:
        - Look up pattern from **digit_patterns[digit]**.
        - Get top_row and bottom_row segment lists.
        - From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_move_to`** (x_position, row 0).
        - For each segment in top_row, use **`lcd_putchar`** with custom char.
        - From **Smart Display**, drag **`lcd_move_to`** (x_position, row 1).
        - For each segment in bottom_row, use **`lcd_putchar`**.
4.  **Initialize RTC**:
    *   from **Smart IO**, drag **`rtc_init`** block.
    *   Set I2C address (typically 0x68 for DS3231).
5.  **Create Main Clock Loop**:
    *   From **Loops**, drag **`while True`** block.
6.  **Read Current Time**:
    *   from **Smart IO**, drag **`rtc_get_time`** block.
    *   Extract **hours** and **minutes**.
7.  **Display Time in Big Digits**:
    *   Clear display.
    *   Extract tens and ones digits:
        - **hour_tens** = hours // 10
        - **hour_ones** = hours % 10
        - **min_tens** = minutes // 10
        - **min_ones** = minutes % 10
    *   Call **draw_big_digit(hour_tens, 0)**  (position 0)
    *   Call **draw_big_digit(hour_ones, 4)**  (position 4)
    *   Display colon ':' at position 8 (both rows).
    *   Call **draw_big_digit(min_tens, 9)**   (position 9)
    *   Call **draw_big_digit(min_ones, 13)**  (position 13)
8.  **Update Every Second**:
    *   from **Smart IO**, drag **`sleep`** for 1 second.
    *   Loop repeats to show updated time.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Screen**: Shows "12:45" but HUGE. Readable from across room.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime
from pico_i2c_lcd import I2cLcd

# Initialize I2C and LCD
i2c = machine.I2C(0, sda=machine.Pin(0), scl=machine.Pin(1), freq=400000)
lcd = I2cLcd(i2c, 0x27, 2, 16)

# Define custom character segments (8 bytes each, 5x8 pixels)
segments = {
    'tl': bytearray([0x07, 0x0F, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F]),  # Top-left
    'tr': bytearray([0x1C, 0x1E, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F]),  # Top-right
    'bl': bytearray([0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x0F, 0x07]),  # Bottom-left
    'br': bytearray([0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1E, 0x1C]),  # Bottom-right
    'tb': bytearray([0x1F, 0x1F, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00]),  # Top bar
    'bb': bytearray([0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x1F, 0x1F]),  # Bottom bar
    'fb': bytearray([0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F, 0x1F]),  # Full bar
    'sp': bytearray([0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00]),  # Space
}

# Load custom characters into LCD (slots 0-7)
lcd.custom_char(0, segments['tl'])
lcd.custom_char(1, segments['tr'])
lcd.custom_char(2, segments['bl'])
lcd.custom_char(3, segments['br'])
lcd.custom_char(4, segments['tb'])
lcd.custom_char(5, segments['bb'])
lcd.custom_char(6, segments['fb'])
lcd.custom_char(7, segments['sp'])

# Big digit patterns: [top_row, bottom_row] using custom char IDs
digits = [
    [[0, 4, 1], [2, 5, 3]],  # 0
    [[7, 1], [7, 6]],         # 1
    [[4, 4, 1], [2, 5, 5]],   # 2
    [[4, 4, 1], [5, 5, 3]],   # 3
    [[0, 5, 1], [7, 7, 6]],   # 4
    [[0, 4, 4], [5, 5, 3]],   # 5
    [[0, 4, 4], [2, 5, 3]],   # 6
    [[4, 4, 1], [7, 7, 6]],   # 7
    [[0, 4, 1], [2, 5, 3]],   # 8
    [[0, 4, 1], [7, 7, 6]],   # 9
]

def draw_big_digit(digit, x):
    """Draw a big (2-row) digit at column x"""
    top, bot = digits[digit]
    
    # Draw top row
    lcd.move_to(x, 0)
    for seg in top:
        lcd.putchar(chr(seg))
    
    # Draw bottom row
    lcd.move_to(x, 1)
    for seg in bot:
        lcd.putchar(chr(seg))

# Simple time tracking (no RTC, just counter for demo)
hours = 12
minutes = 45

while True:
    lcd.clear()
    
    # Display time HH:MM in big digits
    draw_big_digit(hours // 10, 0)     # Hour tens
    draw_big_digit(hours % 10, 4)      # Hour ones
    
    # Draw colon
    lcd.move_to(7, 0)
    lcd.putchar(':')
    lcd.move_to(7, 1)
    lcd.putchar(':')
    
    draw_big_digit(minutes // 10, 9)   # Minute tens
    draw_big_digit(minutes % 10, 13)   # Minute ones
    
    utime.sleep(1)
    
    # Increment time (demo)
    minutes += 1
    if minutes >= 60:
        minutes = 0
        hours += 1
        if hours >= 24:
            hours = 0
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Spacing**: Digits might touch. Add space column.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **3 Rows**: On a 20x4 LCD, make them huge!

## Batch 102: Advanced Data Logging 2 (6-8 (Middle))

## 1. Project 1011: Advanced Data Logging Sensor Setup

### 2. Learning Objective
"RTC Sync". Initialize RTC. If time is 2021 (default), check for `time.txt` file to load last known good time.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Time Persistence**: Pico loses time on power loss.
*   **Fail-safe**: Recovering a "sane" time (even if old) is better than 2021.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **RTC**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **RTC SDA** | GP0 | I2C0 Data (DS3231/DS1307) |
| **RTC SCL** | GP1 | I2C0 Clock |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   from **Smart IO**, drag **`rtc_init`**, **`rtc_datetime_get`**, **`rtc_datetime_set`**
*   From **System & Storage**, drag **`open`**, **`read`**, **`write`**
*   From **Text**, drag **`split`**, **`join`**
*   From **Lists**, drag **`get_item`**
*   From **Math**, drag **`int`**
*   From **Logic**, drag **`if`**, **`try_except`**

### 7. Variables
*   **current_datetime** (RTC datetime tuple)
*   **time_str** (serialized datetime string)
*   **parts** (list of datetime components)
*   **time_tuple** (parsed datetime tuple)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1.  **Initialize RTC**:
    *   from **Smart IO**, drag **`rtc_init`** block.
    *   This creates the RTC object.
2.  **Read Current RTC Time**:
    *   from **Smart IO**, drag **`rtc_datetime_get`** block.
    *   Returns tuple: (year, month, day, weekday, hour, minute, second, subsecond).
    *   Extract year (index 0).
3.  **Check If Time Is Default (Invalid)**:
    *   From **Logic**, drag **`if`** block.
    *   Condition: **year == 2021** (or year < 2023 if current is 2026).
    *   This indicates RTC has never been set or lost power.
4.  **Attempt to Load Saved Time**:
    *   From **Logic**, drag **`try_except`** block.
    *   In try section:
        - From **System & Storage**, drag **`open`** block with filename **"time.txt"**, mode **"r"**.
        - From **System & Storage**, drag **`read`** to get contents.
        - From **Text**, drag **`split`** with delimiter **","** (comma).
        - This gives list of strings: ["2023", "10", "12", "1", "14", "30", "0", "0"].
        - From **Lists**, create new list by converting each string to int.
        - from **Smart IO**, drag **`rtc_datetime_set`** with the parsed tuple.
    *   In except section:
        - Print "No backup time found".
5.  **Periodically Save Current Time**:
    *   In main loop:
        - Get current datetime.
        - Convert tuple to comma-separated string.
        - From **System & Storage**, drag **`open`** with mode **"w"**.
        - Write the time string.
        - Close file.
        - Wait 60 seconds (save every minute).

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Boot**: Restores time from yesterday (roughly).
2.  **Better**: Than 1970 or 2021.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime

rtc = machine.RTC()

# Check if RTC has default/invalid time
current_datetime = rtc.datetime()
if current_datetime[0] == 2021:  # Year is 2021 = not set
    print("RTC not initialized, attempting to load backup...")
    try:
        with open("time.txt", "r") as f:
            time_str = f.read().strip()
            # Parse comma-separated values
            parts = time_str.split(',')
            # Convert to integers
            time_tuple = tuple(int(x) for x in parts)
            # Set RTC to saved time
            rtc.datetime(time_tuple)
            print(f"Loaded time: {time_tuple}")
    except OSError:
        print("No backup time file found")
        # Could set to a default "sane" time here
        # e.g., rtc.datetime((2023, 1, 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0))
else:
    print(f"RTC already set: {current_datetime}")

# Main loop: Save time periodically
while True:
    current_datetime = rtc.datetime()
    
    # Convert tuple to string
    time_str = ','.join(str(x) for x in current_datetime)
    
    # Save to file
    with open("time.txt", "w") as f:
        f.write(time_str)
    
    print(f"Time saved: {current_datetime}")
    utime.sleep(60)  # Save every minute
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Parsing**: String "2023,10,12..." back to tuple (2023, 10, 12...) requires `int()` conversion.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **NTP**: Use WiFi to get real time.

## 1. Project 1012: Advanced Data Logging Data Reader

### 2. Learning Objective
"Analytics". Read 1000 logged temperatures. Calculate Mean, Median, and Mode. Print report.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Statistics**: Central tendencies.
*   **Batch Processing**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **None** | N/A | File processing only |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **System & Storage**, drag **`open`**, **`read`**, **`readlines`**
*   From **Text**, drag **`strip`**, **`split`**, **`float`**
*   From **Lists**, drag **`sort`**, **`length`**, **`get_item`**, **`count`**
*   From **Math**, drag **`sum`**, **`divide`**, **`floor_divide`**, **`max`**
*   From **Loops**, drag **`for_each`**
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set_variable`**
*   From **Logic**, drag **`if`**

### 7. Variables
*   **data** (list of temperature values)
*   **mean** (average temperature)
*   **median** (middle value)
*   **mode** (most frequent value)
*   **mode_count** (frequency of mode)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1.  **Read Temperature Log File**:
    *   From **System & Storage**, drag **`open`** block.
    *   In the **filename** parameter, enter **"temp_log.txt"**.
    *   In the **mode** parameter, enter **"r"** (read mode).
    *   From **System & Storage**, drag **`readlines`** method.
    *   This returns a list where each line is a string.
2.  **Parse Data into Float List**:
    *   From **Variables**, drag **`set variable to`** block.
    *   Name it **data** and initialize to empty list **[]**.
    *   From **Loops**, drag **`for each`** loop.
    *   Loop through each line from the file.
    *   Inside loop:
        - From **Text**, drag **`strip`** to remove whitespace/newlines.
        - From **Text**, drag **`float`** to convert string to number.
        - From **Lists**, drag **`append`** to add to **data** list.
    *   Result: **data** contains 1000 float values.
3.  **Calculate Mean (Average)**:
    *   From **Math**, drag **`sum`** block.
    *   Pass the **data** list to sum all values.
    *   From **Lists**, drag **`length`** block to get count (should be 1000).
    *   From **Math**, drag **`divide`** block.
    *   Calculate: **mean = sum(data) / len(data)**.
    *   From **Variables**, drag **`set variable to`** block.
    *   Store result in variable **mean**.
4.  **Calculate Median (Middle Value)**:
    *   From **Lists**, drag **`sort`** block.
    *   Sort the **data** list in ascending order.
    *   From **Lists**, drag **`length`** to get count.
    *   From **Math**, drag **`floor_divide`**: **middle_index = len(data) // 2**.
    *   From **Lists**, drag **`get_item`** at index **middle_index**.
    *   From **Variables**, store result in **median**.
    *   Note: For even-length lists, this gives the lower-middle value.
5.  **Calculate Mode (Most Frequent Value)**:
    *   From **Variables**, create **frequency_map** (dictionary/dict).
    *   From **Loops**, drag **`for each`** through **data**.
    *   For each value:
        - From **Lists/Dict**, drag **`count`** or manually track frequency.
        - Increment count for this value in **frequency_map**.
    *   From **Math**, drag **`max`** to find highest frequency.
    *   Find the value with that frequency.
    *   From **Variables**, store in **mode** and **mode_count**.
6.  **Print Statistical Report**:
    *   From **Text**, drag **`print`** block.
    *   Display header: **"=== Temperature Analytics Report ==="**.
    *   Print **"Count: "** + **len(data)**.
    *   Print **"Mean: "** + **mean** + **"°C"**.
    *   Print **"Median: "** + **median** + **"°C"**.
    *   Print **"Mode: "** + **mode** + **"°C (appears "** + **mode_count** + **" times)"**.
    *   Format numbers to 2 decimal places for readability.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Console Output**:
    ``
    === Temperature Analytics Report ===
    Count: 1000
    Mean: 24.53°C
    Median: 24.10°C
    Mode: 24.00°C (appears 87 times)
    ``

### 10. Generated Code
``python
# Read and analyze temperature log file

# Read data from file
try:
    with open("temp_log.txt", "r") as f:
        lines = f.readlines()
except OSError:
    print("Error: temp_log.txt not found")
    lines = []  # Use empty list if file missing

# Parse to floats
data = []
for line in lines:
    try:
        value = float(line.strip())
        data.append(value)
    except ValueError:
        pass  # Skip invalid lines

if len(data) == 0:
    print("No valid data found")
else:
    # Calculate Mean
    mean = sum(data) / len(data)
    
    # Calculate Median
    sorted_data = sorted(data)
    n = len(sorted_data)
    if n % 2 == 0:
        # Even number: average of two middle values
        median = (sorted_data[n//2 - 1] + sorted_data[n//2]) / 2
    else:
        # Odd number: exact middle
        median = sorted_data[n//2]
    
    # Calculate Mode (most frequent value)
    frequency = {}
    for value in data:
        # Round to 1 decimal to group similar temps
        rounded = round(value, 1)
        frequency[rounded] = frequency.get(rounded, 0) + 1
    
    mode = max(frequency, key=frequency.get)
    mode_count = frequency[mode]
    
    # Print Report
    print("=" * 40)
    print("Temperature Analytics Report")
    print("=" * 40)
    print(f"Sample Count: {len(data)}")
    print(f"Mean (Average): {mean:.2f}°C")
    print(f"Median (Middle): {median:.2f}°C")
    print(f"Mode (Most Frequent): {mode:.1f}°C (appears {mode_count} times)")
    print(f"Min: {min(data):.2f}°C")
    print(f"Max: {max(data):.2f}°C")
    print(f"Range: {max(data) - min(data):.2f}°C")
    print("=" * 40)
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **RAM**: 1000 integers is fine. 10,000 floats might hit RAM limits on MicroPython.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Stream**: Calc running average without loading all into RAM.

## 1. Project 1013: Advanced Data Logging Threshold Logic

### 2. Learning Objective
"Smart Decimation". If signal changes slowly (Delta < 0.1), Log every 10 mins. If changes fast (Delta > 1.0), Log every 1s.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Adaptive Sampling**: Saving storage space.
*   **Rate of Change**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Sensor**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Sensor** | GP26 | ADC0 |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Sensing**, drag **`read_adc`** or **`read_temperature`**
*   From **System & Storage**, drag **`open`**, **`write`**
*   from **Smart IO**, drag **`sleep`**, **`ticks_ms`**
*   From **Math**, drag **`subtract`**, **`absolute_value`**
*   From **Logic**, drag **`if_elseif`**, **`comparison`**
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set_variable`**
*   From **Text**, drag **`format`**, **`join`**

### 7. Variables
*   **last_val** (previous sensor reading)
*   **curr_val** (current sensor reading)
*   **delta** (absolute change)
*   **log_interval** (seconds between logs)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1.  **Initialize Sensor**:
    *   From **Sensing**, drag **`read_adc`** block.
    *   Set pin to **GP26** (ADC0).
    *   Or use temperature sensor initialization if using DHT/DS18B20.
2.  **Initialize Tracking Variables**:
    *   From **Variables**, drag **`set variable to`** block.
    *   Create **last_val** and set to **0** (or read initial sensor value).
    *   Create **log_interval** and set to **600** (default 10 minutes).
3.  **Create Main Loop**:
    *   From **Loops**, drag **`while True`** block.
    *   All monitoring logic goes inside.
4.  **Read Current Sensor Value**:
    *   From **Sensing**, drag sensor reading block.
    *   Store in variable **curr_val**.
5.  **Calculate Rate of Change**:
    *   From **Math**, drag **`subtract`** block: **curr_val - last_val**.
    *   From **Math**, drag **`absolute_value`** block.
    *   Store in variable **delta** (magnitude of change).
6.  **Determine Appropriate Log Interval**:
    *   From **Logic**, drag **`if_elseif_else`** block.
    *   **First condition**: **delta > 1.0** (fast change):
        - From **Variables**, set **log_interval = 1** (log every 1 second).
        - This captures rapid events in detail.
    *   **Else if**: **delta < 0.1** (very slow change):
        - From **Variables**, set **log_interval = 600** (log every 10 minutes).
        - Saves storage during stable periods.
    *   **Else** (moderate change 0.1 to 1.0):
        - From **Variables**, set **log_interval = 60** (log every 1 minute).
        - Middle ground for gradual changes.
7.  **Write to Log File**:
    *   From **System & Storage**, drag **`open`** block.
    *   Filename: **"sensor_log.txt"**, mode **"a"** (append).
    *   from **Smart IO**, drag **`ticks_ms`** to get timestamp.
    *   From **Text**, drag **`format`** or **`join`**.
    *   Create log entry: **"[timestamp], [curr_val], [delta], [interval]\n"**.
    *   Write to file and close.
8.  **Update Last Value and Sleep**:
    *   From **Variables**, set **last_val = curr_val**.
    *   from **Smart IO**, drag **`sleep`** block.
    *   Sleep duration: **log_interval** seconds.
    *   Loop repeats after this delay.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Steady**: Logging......... (Slow).
2.  **Event**: Logging !!! !!! !!! (Fast).

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime

# Initialize ADC sensor (example: analog temp sensor)
sensor = machine.ADC(26)  # GP26 = ADC0

# Initialize variables
last_val = 0
log_interval = 600  # Default: 10 minutes

# Get initial reading
last_val = sensor.read_u16() / 65535 * 3.3  # Convert to voltage

while True:
    # Read current sensor value
    curr_val = sensor.read_u16() / 65535 * 3.3
    
    # Calculate rate of change
    delta = abs(curr_val - last_val)
    
    # Adaptive interval based on rate of change
    if delta > 1.0:
        log_interval = 1  # Fast change: log every 1 second
    elif delta < 0.1:
        log_interval = 600  # Slow change: log every 10 minutes (600s)
    else:
        log_interval = 60  # Moderate: log every 1 minute
    
    # Write to log file
    timestamp = utime.ticks_ms() // 1000  # Convert to seconds
    with open("sensor_log.txt", "a") as f:
        f.write(f"{timestamp},{curr_val:.3f},{delta:.3f},{log_interval}\n")
    
    print(f"Logged: val={curr_val:.3f}, delta={delta:.3f}, interval={log_interval}s")
    
    # Update last value
    last_val = curr_val
    
    # Sleep until next log
    utime.sleep(log_interval)
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Missed Event**: If sleeping 10 mins, you might miss the start of the spike. Need to poll fast, but LOG slow.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Buffer**: Poll 1s always. Only write to disk if changed.

## 1. Project 1014: Advanced Data Logging Status Monitor

### 2. Learning Objective
"Storage Gauge". Calculate used Flash space vs Total space. Display as "85% Full" bar graph.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Filesystem Stat**: `os.statvfs()`.
*   **Visualization**: Progress bar.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **OLED**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **OLED SDA** | GP4 | I2C0 |
| **OLED SCL** | GP5 | I2C0 |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **System & Storage**, drag **`os_statvfs`**
*   From **Smart Display**, drag **`oled_init`**, **`oled_fill`**, **`oled_rect`**, **`oled_text`**, **`oled_show`**
*   From **Math**, drag **`multiply`**, **`divide`**, **`subtract`**, **`int`**
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set_variable`**

### 7. Variables
*   **stat** (filesystem statistics tuple)
*   **total_bytes** (total flash capacity)
*   **free_bytes** (available flash)
*   **used_percent** (percentage used)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1.  **Initialize OLED Display**:
    *   From **Smart Display**, drag **`oled_init`** block.
    *   Set I2C pins: **SDA = GP4**, **SCL = GP5**.
    *   Set I2C address to **0x3C** (common OLED address).
    *   Set dimensions: **128x64** pixels (standard OLED).
2.  **Get Filesystem Statistics**:
    *   From **System & Storage**, drag **`os_statvfs`** block.
    *   Pass path **"/"** (root filesystem).
    *   Store result in variable **stat**.
    *   This returns tuple: (bsize, frsize, blocks, bfree, bavail, files, ffree, favail, flag, namemax).
3.  **Calculate Total Storage**:
    *   From **Math**, drag **`multiply`** block.
    *   Calculate: **stat[0]** (block size) × **stat[2]** (total blocks).
    *   Store in **total_bytes**.
4.  **Calculate Free Storage**:
    *   From **Math**, drag **`multiply`** block.
    *   Calculate: **stat[0]** × **stat[3]** (free blocks).
    *   Store in **free_bytes**.
5.  **Calculate Used Percentage**:
    *   From **Math**, drag **`subtract`**: **total_bytes - free_bytes**.
    *   From **Math**, drag **`divide`**: result ÷ **total_bytes**.
    *   From **Math**, drag **`multiply`**: result × **100**.
    *   From **Math**, drag **`int`** to round to integer.
    *   Store in **used_percent**.
6.  **Draw Storage Gauge on OLED**:
    *   From **Smart Display**, drag **`oled_fill`** with color **0** (clear screen).
    *   From **Smart Display**, drag **`oled_text`**.
    *   Display title at (10, 10): **"Flash Storage"**.
    *   Display percentage at (10, 30): **"{used_percent}% Used"**.
7.  **Draw Progress Bar**:
    *   From **Smart Display**, drag **`oled_rect`** (outline).
    *   Draw border at (10, 45), width **108**, height **12**.
    *   Calculate fill width: **(used_percent / 100) × 106**.
    *   From **Smart Display**, drag **`oled_rect`** (filled).
    *   Draw filled bar at (11, 46) with calculated width.
8.  **Update Display**:
    *   From **Smart Display**, drag **`oled_show`** to render buffer to screen.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **OLED**: [||||||||  ] 80%.

### 10. Generated Code
### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import os
from ssd1306 import SSD1306_I2C  # Common OLED library

# Initialize I2C and OLED
i2c = machine.I2C(0, sda=machine.Pin(4), scl=machine.Pin(5), freq=400000)
oled = SSD1306_I2C(128, 64, i2c, addr=0x3C)

# Get filesystem statistics
stat = os.statvfs('/')

# Calculate storage
block_size = stat[0]  # f_bsize
total_blocks = stat[2]  # f_blocks
free_blocks = stat[3]  # f_bfree

total_bytes = block_size * total_blocks
free_bytes = block_size * free_blocks
used_bytes = total_bytes - free_bytes
used_percent = int((used_bytes / total_bytes) * 100)

# Clear display
oled.fill(0)

# Display title
oled.text("Flash Storage", 10, 10)

# Display statistics
oled.text(f"{used_percent}% Used", 10, 30)
total_kb = total_bytes // 1024
used_kb = used_bytes // 1024
oled.text(f"{used_kb}/{total_kb}KB", 10, 40)

# Draw progress bar border
oled.rect(10, 50, 108, 10, 1)  # (x, y, width, height, color)

# Draw filled portion
bar_width = int((used_percent / 100) * 106)
oled.fill_rect(11, 51, bar_width, 8, 1)  # Filled bar

# Update display
oled.show()

print(f"Storage: {used_percent}% used ({used_kb}KB / {total_kb}KB)")
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Interpretation**: LittleFS has overhead. 'Free' bytes isn't exact to the byte.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Warning**: Flash LED if > 90% full.

---

## 1. Project 1015: Automated Advanced Data Logging Controller

### 2. Learning Objective
"Auto-Archive". When `log.txt` reaches 10KB, rename it `log_backup_1.txt` and start fresh `log.txt`.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Log Rotation**: Managing disk selection.
*   **File Operations**: Rename, Delete.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **None** | Internal | File ops only |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **System & Storage**, drag **`os_stat`**, **`os_rename`**, **`os_remove`**
*   From **Logic**, drag **`if`**, **`try_except`**, **`comparison`**
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set_variable`**

### 7. Variables
*   **file_size** (current log file size in bytes)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1.  **Check If Log File Exists**:
    *   From the **Logic** category in the block palette, drag the **`try_except`** block into workspace.
    *   This creates error handling structure.
    *   In the **try** section, we'll attempt to read file statistics.
2.  **Get Current Log File Size**:
    *   From the **System & Storage** category, drag the **`os_stat`** block.
    *   In the **path** parameter field, enter the string **"log.txt"**.
    *   This returns a tuple with file information.
    *   From **Lists**, drag **`get_item`** block.
    *   In the **index** field, enter **6** (this is st_size - file size in bytes).
    *   From **Variables**, drag a **`set variable to`** block.
    *   In the variable name field, type **file_size**.
    *   Connect the stat result to this variable.
3.  **Check Size Threshold**:
    *   From the **Logic** category, drag an **`if`** block.
    *   From the **Logic** category, drag a **`comparison`** block (>).
    *   In the left side, select variable **file_size**.
    *   In the right side, from **Math**, drag **`number`** block with value **10000** (10KB threshold in bytes).
    *   Connect this comparison to the if block's condition.
4.  **Delete Old Backup (If Exists)**:
    *   Inside the if block, drag another nested **`try_except`** block from **Logic**.
    *   In the inner try section:
        - From the **System & Storage** category, drag the **`os_remove`** block.
        - In the **path** parameter field, enter **"log_backup_1.txt"**.
        - This deletes the previous backup if it exists.
    *   In the except section:
        - From **Control**, drag **`pass`** block (do nothing - ignore error if file doesn't exist).
5.  **Rename Current Log to Backup**:
    *   Still inside the main if block (after the nested try/except):
    *   From the **System & Storage** category, drag the **`os_rename`** block.
    *   In the **source** parameter field, enter **"log.txt"**.
    *   In the **destination** parameter field, enter **"log_backup_1.txt"**.
    *   This archives the current log file.
6.  **New Log File Auto-Created**:
    *   No explicit action needed.
    *   The next time code writes to **"log.txt"**, the file will be created fresh.
    *   Old data is safely preserved in **"log_backup_1.txt"**.
7.  **Integrate into Logging Loop**:
    *   Place this entire rotation check logic BEFORE each log write operation.
    *   OR run it periodically (e.g., check every 100 writes).
    *   OR call as a function: **check_and_rotate_log()**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Run**: Log grows... 9kb... 10kb.
2.  **Trigger**: `log.txt` disappears, reappears empty. Old data in backup.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import os

try:
    s = os.stat("log.txt")
    if s[6] > 10000:
        try:
            os.remove("log_backup_1.txt")
        except:
            pass
        os.rename("log.txt", "log_backup_1.txt")
        print("Rotated logs")
except:
    pass # file might not exist
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Crash**: Renaming fails if dest file exists in some OS/FS. Need to delete target first.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Timestamp**: Rename to `log_20231012.txt`.

## 1. Project 1016: Advanced Data Logging Safety Trigger

### 2. Learning Objective
"Power Fail Save". Monitor VSYS (Voltage). If drops (Power cut), quickly flush RAM buffers to Disk before death. (Requires Cap?). Just simulate trigger.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Brown-out Detection**.
*   **Data Integrity**: Closing files properly.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Voltage Sensor**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **VSYS Monitor** | GP29 | ADC3 |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Sensing**, drag **`read_adc`**
*   From **System & Storage**, drag **`open`**, **`write`**, **`flush`**, **`close`**
*   From **Logic**, drag **`if`**, **`comparison`**
*   From **Math**, drag **`multiply`**, **`divide`**
*   From **Loops**, drag **`while_true`**, **`break`**

### 7. Variables
*   **vsys_adc** (ADC object for voltage monitoring)
*   **voltage** (current supply voltage)
*   **log_file** (open file handle)
*   **buffer** (data pending write)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1.  **Initialize VSYS Monitoring**:
    *   From **Sensing**, drag **`ADC`** init.
    *   Set pin to **GP29** (VSYS/3 on Pico).
    *   VSYS is divided by 3, so actual voltage = ADC reading × 3.
2.  **Open Log File**:
    *   From **System & Storage**, drag **`open`**.
    *   Filename: **"data.log"**, mode **"a"** (append).
    *   Store handle in **log_file**.
3.  **Main Logging Loop**:
    *   From **Loops**, drag **`while True`**.
    *   Normal operation: collect sensor data.
4.  **Monitor Voltage Continuously**:
    *   Inside loop, read **GP29** ADC.
    *   Convert to voltage: **(ADC value / 65535) × 3.3 × 3**.
    *   Store in **voltage**.
5.  **Detect Power Loss**:
    *   From **Logic**, drag **`if`**.
    *   Condition: **voltage < 4.0** (below safe threshold).
6.  **Emergency Save**:
    *   Inside if:
        - Write buffered data to file immediately.
        - From **System & Storage**, drag **`flush`** to force disk write.
        - From **System & Storage**, drag **`close`** to finalize file.
        - Print warning message.
        - From **Loops**, drag **`break`** to exit loop.
7.  **Normal Write** (else branch):
    *   Accumulate data in buffer.
    *   Write to file periodically.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Normal**: Storing in variable (fast).
2.  **Unplug**: "Saving..." (hopefully catches it).

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime

# Initialize VSYS ADC (GP29 on Pico)
# Note: VSYS is divided by 3 resistors, so multiply reading by 3
vsys_adc = machine.ADC(29)

# Conversion factor for VSYS
conversion_factor = 3.3 / 65535 * 3  # ADC to voltage, then × 3 for divider

# Open log file
log_file = open("power_monitor.log", "a")

print("Power fail monitoring active...")

try:
    while True:
        # Read VSYS voltage
        adc_reading = vsys_adc.read_u16()
        voltage = adc_reading * conversion_factor
        
        # Check for power drop
        if voltage < 4.0:  # Threshold for power loss
            print("⚠️ POWER LOW! Emergency save...")
            
            # Flush any buffered data
            log_file.write(f"POWER FAILURE DETECTED at {utime.ticks_ms()}ms\n")
            log_file.flush()  # Force write to flash
            log_file.close()  # Properly close file
            
            print("Data saved. System halting.")
            break  # Exit loop
        
        # Normal operation: log data
        log_file.write(f"{utime.ticks_ms()},{voltage:.2f}V\n")
        
        utime.sleep(0.1)  # Check every 100ms
        
except KeyboardInterrupt:
    log_file.close()
    print("Monitoring stopped.")
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Speed**: Writing to flash takes ms. Caps usually needed to hold charge 100ms+.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Capacitor**: Add 1000uF cap to VSYS.

## 1. Project 1017: Advanced Data Logging Data Logger

### 2. Learning Objective
"Event Tagging". Press Button A to insert marker `*** EVENT START ***` into the stream of sensor data.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Annotation**: Marking data for human review.
*   **Interrupts**: User input during logging.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Button**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button** | GP15 | Event trigger |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Inputs**, drag **`button_init`**, **`button_irq`**
*   From **System & Storage**, drag **`open`**, **`write`**
*   From **Functions**, drag **`define_function`**
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set_variable`**

### 7. Variables
*   **button** (button input object)
*   **event_count** (number of events marked)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1.  **Initialize Button**:
    *   From **Inputs**, drag **`Pin`** init.
    *   Set pin **GP15**, mode **IN**, pull **PULL_UP**.
2.  **Define Interrupt Handler**:
    *   From **Functions**, drag **`define_function`**.
    *   Name: **mark_event**, parameter: **pin**.
3.  **Handler Logic**:
    *   Open log file in append mode.
    *   Write marker: **"*** EVENT START ***"**.
    *   Close file.
4.  **Attach Interrupt**:
    *   Call **button.irq()** with handler.
    *   Trigger: **IRQ_FALLING** (button press).
5.  **Main Loop Continues Normal Logging**:
    *   Sensor data logged continuously.
    *   Button press inserts marker asynchronously.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **System & Storage**:
    *   23.4
    *   23.5
    *   *** EVENT START ***
    *   23.6

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime

# Initialize button
btn = machine.Pin(15, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_UP)

# Event counter
event_count = 0

def mark_event(pin):
    """Interrupt handler - insert marker into log"""
    global event_count
    event_count += 1
    
    with open("sensor_log.txt", "a") as f:
        timestamp = utime.ticks_ms()
        f.write(f"\n*** EVENT {event_count} START - Time:{timestamp}ms ***\n")
    
    print(f"Event {event_count} marked")

# Attach interrupt (falling edge = button press)
btn.irq(trigger=machine.Pin.IRQ_FALLING, handler=mark_event)

print("Event tagging active. Press button to mark events.")

# Main logging loop
while True:
    # Simulate sensor reading
    sensor_value = 23.5  # Replace with actual sensor
    
    with open("sensor_log.txt", "a") as f:
        f.write(f"{utime.ticks_ms()},{sensor_value:.1f}\n")
    
    utime.sleep(1)  # Log every second
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **File Conflict**: IRQ writing to file while Main Loop writes to file? Filesystem object is likely not re-entrant. Set a flag, write in main loop.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Voice Note**: (Advanced) Record audio clip? (Too hard).

7.  **Create Logging Loop** (optional):

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import utime

# Simulate sensor reading (replace with actual sensor)
def read_sensor():
    return 24.5  # Example temperature

# Open SQL export file
with open("export.sql", "a") as f:
    # Write table creation statement (do this once)
    # f.write("CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sensors (timestamp INTEGER, value REAL);\n")
    
    # Log 10 samples for demonstration
    for i in range(10):
        # Read sensor
        sensor_value = read_sensor()
        
        # Get timestamp
        timestamp = utime.ticks_ms()
        
        # Format SQL INSERT statement
        sql_query = f"INSERT INTO sensors (timestamp, value) VALUES ({timestamp}, {sensor_value});\n"
        
        # Write to file
        f.write(sql_query)
        
        print(f"Logged: {sql_query.strip()}")
        
        # Wait before next reading

print("SQL export complete. File: export.sql")
``
