## 1. Project 1056: Advanced Laser Tripwire Safety Trigger

### 2. Learning Objective
"Machine Guard". Light Curtain. If hand breaks any of 4 parallel beams -> E-Stop Motor.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **AND Logic**: Safe only if A AND B AND C AND D are aligned.
*   **Industrial ISO Standards** (Concept only).

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **4 Lasers/LDRs**
*   **Motor**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Laser 1-4** | GP14-17 | Parallel beams |
| **LDR 1-4** | GP26-29 | Detectors |
| **Motor Relay** | GP20 | E-Stop |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Logic**, drag **`and`**, **`if_all`**
*   From **Pins**, drag **`digital_write`**

### 7. Variables
*   **is_safe**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1.  **Check**: `is_safe = L1 and L2 and L3 and L4`.
2.  **Act**:
    *   `if is_safe: Motor.on()`.
    *   `else: Motor.off()`.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Clear**: Machine runs.
2.  **Hand In**: Machine stops instantly.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
while True:
    # Assuming Active HIGH (Light = 1)
    if l1.value() and l2.value() and l3.value() and l4.value():
        relay.on()
    else:
        relay.off()
        print("UNSAFE")
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Beam Spread**: Lasers dot size. Aligning 4 is tedious.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Reset**: Require button press to restart after breach (Latch).

## 1. Project 1058: Smart Advanced Laser Tripwire System

### 2. Learning Objective
"Li-Fi" (Simple). Send ASCII Text byte by byte. 0=Laser Off, 1=Laser On. 300 baud. (Slow but functional).

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Free Space Optics**.
*   **UART over GPIO**: Bit-banging Serial.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Laser**
*   **LDR**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Laser/TX** | GP0 | UART TX |
| **LDR/RX** | GP1 | UART RX |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Communication**, drag **`uart_init`**, **`uart_write`**
*   From **Communication**, drag **`uart_read`**

### 7. Variables
*   **msg**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1.  **Init**: UART0 on pins GP0/GP1.
2.  **Tx**: `uart.write("Hello")`. (Laser blinks).
3.  **Rx**: `if uart.any(): print(uart.read())`.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Setup**: Aim Laser at LDR across room.
2.  **Type**: "Secret Code".
3.  **Receive**: "Secret Code".

### 10. Generated Code
``python
u = machine.UART(0, baudrate=300, tx=machine.Pin(0), rx=machine.Pin(1))

# On the TX Pico
u.write("Hello World")

# On the RX Pico
print(u.read())
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Inversion**: UART is Idle High. Laser acts as Idle Low (Off). Need to invert signal or logic. (Usually UART Idle = 3.3V. Laser Off = 0V. Need NOT gate or `invert=True` in UART init if supported).

### 12. Try This Next

## 1. Project 1059: Advanced Laser Tripwire Calibration Tool

### 2. Learning Objective
"Alignment Audio". Pitch of buzzer rises as signal intensity increases. Blind alignment helper.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Sonification**: Data to Sound.
*   **Analog Intensity**: Laser center is brighter than edges.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **LDR**
*   **Buzzer**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LDR** | GP26 | ADC0 |
| **Buzzer PWM** | GP15 | Piezo |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Motion & Motors**, drag **`set_pwm_freq`**
*   From **Math**, drag **`map_range`**

### 7. Variables
*   **val, freq**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize Display**:
    *   Show Bar Graph or Dial.
2. **Loop**:
    *   Read ADC.
    *   Map Value (0-65535) to Signal Strength % (0-100).
    *   **Peak Hold**: Keep Max value seen for 1 second.
    *   **Beep**: Tone pitch = ADC Value (Higher = Better alignment).
3. **Tuning**:
    *   User adjusts mirror/laser angle.
    *   Tries to maximize Pitch/Bar graph.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Wiggle Laser**: "Beep... BEEP!... Beep".
2.  **Lock**: Tighten screw at max BEEP.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime

adc = machine.ADC(26)
buzzer = machine.PWM(machine.Pin(15))

print("Alignment Mode")
max_val = 0

while True:
    val = adc.read_u16()
    
    # 1. Audio Feedback (Pitch)
    # 0 -> 100Hz, 65000 -> 2000Hz
    freq = 100 + int((val / 65535) * 2000)
    buzzer.freq(freq)
    buzzer.duty_u16(10000) # Low volume
    
    # 2. Visual Feedback (Console)
    bars = int((val / 65535) * 50)
    graph = "#" * bars
    print(f"Signal: {val} [{graph}]")
    
    if val > max_val:
        max_val = val
        print("NEW PEAK!")
        
    utime.sleep(0.1)
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Saturation**: If ambient light saturates LDR, laser adds nothing. Shade the LDR tube.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Silent**: Use LED brightness instead.

## 1. Project 1060: Advanced Advanced Laser Tripwire Prototype

### 2. Learning Objective
"Harp". 5 Lasers. Blocking a beam plays a specific Note. (Laser Harp).

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Polyphony**: (Maybe simple monophonic here).
*   **Instrument Design**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **5 Lasers/LDRs**
*   **Buzzer**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LDR 1** | GP26 | ADC0 - C note |
| **LDR 2** | GP27 | ADC1 - D note |
| **LDR 3** | GP28 | ADC2 - E note |
| **Buzzer PWM** | GP15 | Audio out |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Audio**, drag **`play_tone`**, **`set_frequency`**
*   From **Lists**, drag **`get_item`**

### 7. Variables
*   **notes**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize Hardware**:
    *   Init **Buzzer** on GP15.
    *   Init 5 LDRs (GP26-29 + Mux? or Digital Pins).
    *   Wait: Pico only has 3 ADCs.
    *   **pivot**: Use **Digital Logic**. Use strong laser + voltage divider. Dark = High (1), Hit = Low (0).
    *   Init GP16-GP20 as Digital Inputs (Pull Up).
2. **Define Notes**:
    *   List **freqs** = [262, 294, 330, 349, 392] (C4-G4).
3. **Loop**:
    *   Scan all 5 inputs.
    *   If **Pin[i] == 1** (Beam Broken/Shadow):
        *   Play Tone **freqs[i]**.
        *   Set **playing = True**.
        *   Break loop (Priority to lowest string).
    *   If no beams broken:
        *   Stop Tone.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Play**: Hand waves through beams.
2.  **Sound**: Do-Re-Mi scale.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime

# Hardware
buzzer = machine.PWM(machine.Pin(15))
# Using Digital Inputs for "Beams" (assuming Schmidt trigger or clear signal)
pins = [machine.Pin(i, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_UP) for i in range(16, 21)]

# C Major Scale
notes = [262, 294, 330, 349, 392]

print("Laser Harp Ready - Break Beams to Play")

current_note = 0

while True:
    active_beam = -1
    
    # Check all beams
    for i in range(5):
        # 0 = Connected (Light), 1 = Broken (Shadow)?
        # Standard LDR divider usually High when Dark
        if pins[i].value() == 1: 
            active_beam = i
            break # Monophonic - take first one
            
    if active_beam != -1:
        # Play Note
        freq = notes[active_beam]
        buzzer.freq(freq)
        buzzer.duty_u16(10000) # Volume
        current_note = freq
    else:
        # Silence
        buzzer.duty_u16(0)
        
    utime.sleep(0.02) # Low latency
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Smoke**: Laser beams are invisible without smoke/fog. Visuals are half the fun.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **MIDI**: Send MIDI to PC for real synth sounds.

## Batch 107: Advanced Radar Screen 2 (6-8 (Middle))

## 1. Project 1061: Advanced Radar Screen Sensor Setup

### 2. Learning Objective
"Servo Sweep". Use PIO or Timer to move servo smoothly in background while Main Loop reads Ultrasonic. Decouple movement.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Asynchronous Tasks**: Doing two things at once.
*   **Timer Interrupts**: Periodic updates.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Servo**
*   **HC-SR04**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Servo PWM** | GP0 | Sweep control |
| **HC-SR04 Trig** | GP2 | Ultrasonic trigger |
| **HC-SR04 Echo** | GP3 | Echo return |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   from **Smart IO**, drag **`set_timer`**, **`Timer.PERIODIC`**
*   From **Motion & Motors**, drag **`servo_angle`**

### 7. Variables
*   **angle, direction**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize Hardware**:
    *   Init **Servo** on GP0.
    *   Init **HC-SR04** (Trig GP2, Echo GP3).
2. **Setup Timer**:
    *   Create **Timer** object.
    *   Init periodic callback (freq=20Hz).
    *   **Callback Logic**:
        *   Update **angle** by **step** (+5 or -5).
        *   If angle > 180 or < 0, reverse **step**.
        *   Move Servo to **angle**.
3. **Main Loop**:
    *   Wait 100ms.
    *   Measure Distance.
    *   Print "Angle: [angle], Dist: [distance]".

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Servo**: Sweeps back and forth automatically.
2.  **Console**: Prints distance whenever ready. No "Stop-Measure-Move" jerkiness.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime
from servo import Servo # Assuming library or simple PWM
from hcsr04 import HCSR04 # Assuming library

servo_pin = machine.Pin(0)
pwm = machine.PWM(servo_pin)
pwm.freq(50)

sonar = HCSR04(trigger_pin=2, echo_pin=3)

current_angle = 90
step = 2

def set_angle(angle):
    duty = int((angle / 180 * 8000) + 1000)
    pwm.duty_u16(duty)

def sweep_tick(t):
    global current_angle, step
    current_angle += step
    if current_angle >= 180 or current_angle <= 0:
        step = -step
    set_angle(current_angle)

# Start Sweep in Background
tim = machine.Timer()
tim.init(freq=20, mode=machine.Timer.PERIODIC, callback=sweep_tick)

print("Radar Sweep Active")

while True:
    try:
        dist = sonar.distance_cm()
        # Note: current_angle changes async!
        print(f"Ang: {current_angle} | Dist: {dist} cm")
        utime.sleep(0.1)
    except OSError:
        pass
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Conflict**: IRQ taking too long blocks sonar echo?

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Sync**: Trigger sonar ON servo step?

---

## 1. Project 1062: Advanced Radar Screen Data Reader

### 2. Learning Objective
"Target Lock". If object found at 45 deg, stop sweeping. Adjust servo slightly L/R to keep object centered (Tracking).

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Control Loop**: Visual Servoing (with sonar).
*   **Tracking**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Servo**
*   **HC-SR04**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Servo PWM** | GP0 | Position control |
| **HC-SR04 Trig** | GP2 | Trigger |
| **HC-SR04 Echo** | GP3 | Echo |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Motion & Motors**, drag **`servo_angle`**
*   From **Sensing**, drag **`read_distance`**
*   From **Logic**, drag **`if_else`**

### 7. Variables
*   **pos_x** (Servo angle)
*   **target_dist**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize**:
    *   Servo at 90 (Center).
    *   Target Distance = 20cm (Lock point).
2. **Loop**:
    *   Measure **dist**.
    *   If **dist < 30cm** (Object detected):
        *   **Scan Logic**:
            *   Turn Left (-5 deg). Measure.
            *   Turn Right (+5 deg). Measure.
            *   Move to angle with **Minimum Distance** (Closest point).
    *   If **dist > 50cm**:
        *   Resume Sweep Mode (Search).

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Search**: Scanning...
2.  **Found**: Locks onto object.
3.  **Move Object**: Servo turns to follow it.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime

servo = machine.PWM(machine.Pin(0))
servo.freq(50)
trig = machine.Pin(2, machine.Pin.OUT)
echo = machine.Pin(3, machine.Pin.IN)

def get_dist():
    trig.low()
    utime.sleep_us(2)
    trig.high()
    utime.sleep_us(5)
    trig.low()
    try:
        t = machine.time_pulse_us(echo, 1, 30000)
        if t < 0: return 100
        return (t / 2) / 29.1
    except: return 100

def move(ang):
    duty = int((ang / 180 * 8000) + 1000)
    servo.duty_u16(duty)

pos = 90
move(pos)

print("Turret Active")

while True:
    d = get_dist()
    
    if d < 30: # Object Near - TRACK
        print(f"Subject at {d:.1f}cm")
        
        # Simple Hill Climbing / Dithering
        # Look Left
        move(pos - 10)
        utime.sleep(0.1)
        d_left = get_dist()
        
        # Look Right
        move(pos + 10)
        utime.sleep(0.1)
        d_right = get_dist()
        
        # Decide
        if d_left < d and d_left < d_right:
            pos -= 10
        elif d_right < d and d_right < d_left:
            pos += 10
        else:
            move(pos) # Return center
            
        pos = max(0, min(180, pos))
        
    else: # Search Mode
        pos = (pos + 20) % 180
        move(pos)
        utime.sleep(0.1)
``

### 7. Variables
*   **pos**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1.  **Scan**: Till `dist < 20cm`.
2.  **Track**:
    *   Measure `C`. Move L. Measure `L`.
    *   Measure `R`. Move R.
    *   Go towards min distance? Or use edge detection?
    *   Simplest: Sweep small arc +/- 10 deg.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Search**: Head moves 0-180.
2.  **Found**: Head stares at object. Move object, head follows.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
# Simplified "Bang-Bang" tracker?
# Hard with single pixel (sonar). 
# Edge tracking might works: Find Left Edge, Find Right Edge, Center = (L+R)/2.
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Jitter**: Sonar is noisy. Tracker shakes violently. Need hysteresis/smoothing.

## 1. Project 1063: Advanced Radar Screen Threshold Logic

### 2. Learning Objective
"Size filter". Detect width. If object seen at 40, 41, 42, 43 deg -> Small. If 30-60 deg -> Large. Ignore Small.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Feature Extraction**: Width = Anglular Size * Distance.
*   **Filtering**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Servo**
*   **HC-SR04**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Servo PWM** | GP0 | Sweep |
| **HC-SR04 Trig** | GP2 | Distance measure |
| **HC-SR04 Echo** | GP3 | Echo receive |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Math**, drag **`subtract`**, **`multiply`**
*   From **Lists**, drag **`append`**, **`count`**

### 7. Variables
*   **start_angle, end_angle**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1.  **Sweep**: Record `detected` (True/False) per degree. `[0,0,1,1,1,0,0]`.
2.  **Count**: Run length of 1s.
3.  **Calc**: Width deg.
4.  **Logic**: `if width > 15: print("BIG")`.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Pencil**: Ignored.
2.  **Box**: "Large Object Detected".

### 10. Generated Code
``python
hits = []
for ang in range(0, 180, 5):
    servo.move(ang)
    d = sonar.distance_cm()
    hits.append(1 if d < 30 else 0)
    utime.sleep(0.05)
    
# Process hits
width = count_consecutive(hits) * 5
print(f"Angle Width: {width}")
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Distance**: Angular width depends on distance. Needs trig to get real width in cm.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Real Width**: `W = 2 * D * tan(theta/2)`.

## 1. Project 1066: Advanced Radar Screen Safety Trigger

### 2. Learning Objective
"Anti-Creep". Alarm if object slowly gets closer over multiple scans (Stalker logic).

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Trend Analysis**: Tracking `d/dt`.
*   **Threat Detection**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Scanner**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Servo PWM** | GP0 | Scanner head |
| **HC-SR04 Trig** | GP2 | Distance |
| **HC-SR04 Echo** | GP3 | Echo |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Lists**, drag **`append`**, **`pop`**
*   From **Logic**, drag **`all`**, **`for_loop`**

### 7. Variables
*   **history**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize Scanner**:
    *   Setup Servo and Sonar.
    *   Create **history** list (size 5).
2. **Monitor Loop**:
    *   Measure distance **d**.
    *   Add **d** to **history**. Keep size 5.
    *   **Trend Analysis**:
        *   Check if `history[4] < history[3] < history[2]...`.
        *   Calculate Velocity: `V = (d_new - d_old) / time`.
    *   **Logic**:
        *   If `d < 20cm` (Collision Imminent):
            *   ALARM (Print "STOP!").
        *   If `Velocity < -10 cm/s` (Approaching Fast):
            *   WARNING (Print "Approaching").
3. **Loop**:
    *   Run at 10Hz.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Static**: Silent.
2.  **Moving Closer**: Beep beep BEEP BEEP.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime

# Hardware Setup (Sonar/Servo/Buzzer)
trig = machine.Pin(2, machine.Pin.OUT)
echo = machine.Pin(3, machine.Pin.IN)
buzzer = machine.Pin(15, machine.Pin.OUT)

def get_dist():
    trig.low(); utime.sleep_us(2); trig.high(); utime.sleep_us(5); trig.low()
    try:
        t = machine.time_pulse_us(echo, 1, 30000)
        if t < 0: return 200
        return (t / 2) / 29.1
    except: return 200

history = [200] * 5
print("Proximity Guard Active")

while True:
    d = get_dist()
    
    # Update History
    history.pop(0)
    history.append(d)
    
    # Calculate Velocity (cm per sample interval)
    # 100ms interval -> V = delta_d / 0.1
    delta = history[4] - history[0]
    velocity = delta / 0.5 # 5 samples * 0.1s
    
    msg = f"Dist: {d:.1f}cm | Vel: {velocity:.1f} cm/s"
    
    if d < 20:
        print("CRITICAL PROXIMITY! STOP!", msg)
        buzzer.on()
    elif velocity < -20: # Approaching faster than 20cm/s
        print("FAST APPROACH DETECTED", msg)
        # Beep pulse
        buzzer.on(); utime.sleep(0.05); buzzer.off()
    else:
        print("Safe", msg)
        buzzer.off()
        
    utime.sleep(0.1)
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Noise**: 1 bad reading breaks the detected trend. Needs line fitting.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Velocity**: Calculate approach speed m/s.

---

## 1. Project 1067: Advanced Radar Screen Data Logger

### 2. Learning Objective
"Room Profile". Scan empty room. Save "Reference" array. Later, scan again. Report differences (New object).

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Calibration / Tare**.
*   **Change Detection**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Scanner**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Servo PWM** | GP0 | Scanner |
| **HC-SR04 Trig** | GP2 | Sonar trigger |
| **HC-SR04 Echo** | GP3 | Echo |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Lists**, drag **`compare`**, **`subtract`**
*   From **System & Storage**, drag **`read_file`**, **`write_file`**

### 7. Variables
*   **ref_map, curr_map**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize**:
    *   Setup Scanner HW.
    *   Check for existing `ref_map.txt`.
    *   If not found, run **Calibration Sweep**.
2. **Calibration Sweep**:
    *   Scan 0-180 deg (Step 2).
    *   Save distances to file.
3. **Monitor Loop**:
    *   Load `ref_map`.
    *   Run new Sweep -> `curr_map`.
    *   **Compare**:
        *   Iterate angles.
        *   `diff = Reference - Current`.
        *   If `diff > 30` (Closer than ref): **Intruder**.
        *   If `diff < -30` (Further than ref): **Missing Object**.
    *   Print Report.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Learn**: Scans room.
2.  **Add Box**: Scans... "Change detected at 45 deg".

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime
import os

# Assume get_dist() and set_angle() exist
# ... setup code ...

def sweep():
    data = []
    for ang in range(0, 181, 5):
        set_angle(ang)
        utime.sleep(0.05)
        data.append(get_dist())
    return data

filename = "room_ref.txt"
ref_data = []

# Load or Calibrate
try:
    with open(filename, "r") as f:
        line = f.readline()
        # Parse "100,102,99..."
        ref_data = [float(x) for x in line.split(",")]
    print("Reference Map Loaded")
except:
    print("Calibrating Room...")
    ref_data = sweep()
    with open(filename, "w") as f:
        f.write(",".join([str(x) for x in ref_data]))
    print("Reference Saved")

while True:
    print("Scanning...")
    curr = sweep()
    
    for i, d in enumerate(curr):
        ang = i * 5
        ref = ref_data[i]
        diff = ref - d # Positive if object is CLOSER
        
        if diff > 30:
            print(f"Angle {ang}: INTRUDER (Ref:{ref:.0f}, Now:{d:.0f})")
        elif diff < -30:
            print(f"Angle {ang}: MISSING ITEM")
            
    utime.sleep(2)
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Drift**: Temp changes affect speed of sound -> distance drifts. Ref invalid after temperature swing.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Update**: Slowly update background Ref (Learning).

## 1. Project 1068: Smart Advanced Radar Screen System

### 2. Learning Objective
"Multi-static". Pico A emits Pulse. Pico B and C receive echo. Triangulate position? (Hard). Try: Pico A tells Pico B "Ping now".

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Sensor Network**.
*   **Synchronization**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **2 Picos**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Pico A Sync** | GP0 | Trigger signal to B |
| **Pico A Trig** | GP2 | Ultrasonic TX |
| **Pico B Sync** | GP0 | Receives trigger |
| **Pico B Echo** | GP3 | Receives echo |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Logic**, drag **`trigger`**, **`wait_for`**
*   from **Smart IO**, drag **`ticks_us`**, **`ticks_diff`**

### 7. Variables
*   **t_flight**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Pico A (Tx)**:
    *   Setup GP0 as **Sync Out** (Digital).
    *   Setup Sonar Trig.
    *   Loop:
        *   Pulse Sync High -> Low.
        *   Immediately Fire Sonar Ping.
        *   Wait 1s.
2. **Pico B (Rx)**:
    *   Setup GP0 as **Sync In** (Interrupt/Polling).
    *   Setup GP3 (Echo input) - No Trig needed.
    *   Loop:
        *   Wait for Sync Rise.
        *   Start Timer (`t0`).
        *   Wait for Echo Rise (Ping reached B).
        *   Stop Timer (`t1`).
        *   Calculate Distance `d = (t1-t0) * speed`.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Ping**: Complex geometry. Elliptical locus.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
# Pico B Code (Receiver)
import machine
import utime

sync_pin = machine.Pin(0, machine.Pin.IN) # From Pico A
echo_pin = machine.Pin(3, machine.Pin.IN) # From Receiver Module

print("Bistatic Receiver Ready")

while True:
    # 1. Wait for Sync
    while sync_pin.value() == 0:
        pass
        
    start_time = utime.ticks_us()
    
    # 2. Wait for Sound Wave (Direct path or Reflected?)
    # Assuming we want Reflected. Direct path is shortest.
    # Receiver will trigger on first sound (Direct).
    
    while echo_pin.value() == 0:
        if utime.ticks_diff(utime.ticks_us(), start_time) > 100000: # Timeout
            break
            
    arrival_time = utime.ticks_us()
    flight_time = utime.ticks_diff(arrival_time, start_time)
    
    if flight_time < 100000:
        # Distance = Total Path (A->Obj->B)
        total_dist_cm = flight_time / 29.1
        print(f"Total Path: {total_dist_cm:.1f} cm")
    
    # Wait for reset
    utime.sleep(0.5)
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Sync**: Wire delay is negligible. Radio sync has latency.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **2 Transmitters**: Interference? Only if firing same time.

## 1. Project 1069: Advanced Radar Screen Calibration Tool

### 2. Learning Objective
"Beam Width". Map the effective cone. Place object. Step servo 1 deg at a time. Record angles where object is visible. Detect 30deg cone.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Sensor Characterization**.
*   **Resolution vs Detection**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Scanner**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Servo PWM** | GP0 | Scanner |
| **HC-SR04** | GP2/3 | Measure |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Math**, drag **`abs`**, **`min`**
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set`**

### 7. Variables

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Setup**: Place a thin pole at approx 0 degrees (Center of servo?). Actually 90.
2. **Coarse Scan**:
    *   Sweep 0-180.
    *   Find angles where dist < 50cm.
3. **Fine Scan**:
    *   Iterate across the "Hit" angles.
    *   Find the **center** of the cluster.
    *   Measure width of the cluster in degrees.
    *   Calculate effective Beam Width = Measured Width - Actual Object Width(negligible).
4. **Report**: "Sensor Beam Width is approx 15 degrees".

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Test**: Object is 2cm wide.
2.  **Result**: Sensor "sees" it from angle 80 to 95. Beam width = 15 deg.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
# Assuming scan function
hits = []
for ang in range(0, 180):
    set_angle(ang)
    utime.sleep(0.05)
    d = get_dist()
    if d < 50: hits.append(ang)

if len(hits) > 0:
    start = hits[0]
    end = hits[-1]
    width = end - start
    print(f"Object visible from {start} to {end} (Width: {width} deg)")
    
    # Characterization
    # HC-SR04 usually has 15-30 deg cone
    center = (start + end) / 2
    print(f"Center at {center}")
else:
    print("No Object")
``
| **Servo PWM** | GP0 | Fine angle control |

## Batch 108: Advanced Weather Station 2 (6-8 (Middle))

## 1. Project 1071: Advanced Weather Station Sensor Setup

### 2. Learning Objective
"External Probe". DS18B20 (OneWire). Setup OneWire bus. Scan for addresses (ROM codes). Read Temp from specific address.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Bus Enumeration**: Finding devices on a bus.
*   **OneWire Protocol**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **DS18B20**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **DS18B20 Data** | GP16 | OneWire (needs 4.7k pullup) |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart Sensors**, drag **`onewire_init`**, **`ds18b20_read`**
*   From **Loops**, drag **`for_each`**

### 7. Variables
*   **roms**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize Protocol**:
    *   Initialize **OneWire** Bus on GP16.
    *   Initialize **DS18X20** Driver using the bus.
2. **Scan**:
    *   Call `scan()`. Stores list of **ROM codes**.
    *   Print "Found [len] sensors".
    *   Print scan result (Hex addresses).
3. **Loop**:
    *   **Convert**: Send `convert_temp()` command (Broadcast to all).
    *   **Wait**: Sleep 750ms (Required for 12-bit precision).
    *   **Read**: Iterate through list of ROMs.
        *   Read temp for each ROM.
    *   **Smart Display**: Print "Sensor [ID]: [Temp] C".

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Start**: "Found: 28ff4a...".
2.  **Loop**: "Temp: 24.5C".

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime
import onewire
import ds18x20

# Setup OneWire Line on GP16
# IMPORTANT: 4.7k Pull-up Resistor required between GP16 and 3.3V
dat = machine.Pin(16)
ds = ds18x20.DS18X20(onewire.OneWire(dat))

# 1. Scan for devices
print("Scanning OneWire Bus...")
roms = ds.scan()
print(f"Found {len(roms)} devices: {roms}")

while True:
    if len(roms) > 0:
        # 2. Request Conversion (All sensors)
        ds.convert_temp()
        
        # 3. Wait (Max conversion time for 12-bit is 750ms)
        utime.sleep_ms(750)
        
        # 4. Read Results
        for rom in roms:
            try:
                temp = ds.read_temp(rom)
                # Convert bytearray rom to hex string for display
                rom_id = ''.join('{:02x}'.format(x) for x in rom)
                print(f"Sensor {rom_id}: {temp:.2f} C")
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"Read Error: {e}")
                
    else:
        print("No sensors found - Check Pull-up Resistor")
        
    utime.sleep(1)
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Pull-up**: 4.7k resistor required between Data and VCC.
*   **Smart IO**: Must wait 750ms for conversion.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Multiple**: Connect 2 probes. Read both.

## 1. Project 1072: Advanced Weather Station Data Reader

### 2. Learning Objective
"Wind Dir". wind vane uses 8 reed switches + resistors (Voltage Divider). Read ADC. Match voltage to Direction (N, NE, E...).

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Analog Keypad logic**: Using different R values to distinguish switches on 1 wire.
*   **Lookup Table**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Wind Vane (Resistor Ladder)**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Wind Vane Signal** | GP26 | ADC0 (voltage divider) |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Logic**, drag **`if_elseif`**, **`switch_case`**
*   From **Sensing**, drag **`read_adc`**

### 7. Variables
*   **adc_val, direction**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize ADC**:
    *   Setup ADC0 (GP26).
2. **Define Lookup Table**:
    *   Create logic for 8 directions (N, NE, E, SE, S, SW, W, NW).
    *   Voltage Divider Vane produces distinct voltages.
    *   Example thresholds (for 3.3V supply and specific R values):
        *   N: ~3.0V
        *   E: ~1.5V
        *   etc. (Depends on specific hardware, e.g., Sparkfun Weather Meter).
3. **Loop**:
    *   Read ADC (0-65535).
    *   Compare against thresholds ranges.
    *   Print Direction String.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Rotate**: Voltage changes stepwise.
2.  **Output**: N -> NE -> E...

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime

adc = machine.ADC(26)

# Map for typical resistor ladder wind vane (Values to be calibrated)
# Need ranges because ADC fluctuates slightly
def get_direction(val):
    # Example values for 16-direction vane (SparkFun)
    # Using larger bands for simplicity
    if val < 5000: return "Unknown"
    if val < 8000: return "W"
    if val < 15000: return "NW"
    if val < 24000: return "N" 
    if val < 32000: return "SW"
    if val < 45000: return "NE" 
    if val < 55000: return "S"
    if val < 62000: return "SE" 
    return "E"

print("Wind Vane Active")

while True:
    raw = adc.read_u16()
    direction = get_direction(raw)
    
    # Print voltage for calibration
    volts = (raw / 65535) * 3.3
    print(f"Raw: {raw} ({volts:.2f}V) -> {direction}")
    
    utime.sleep(0.5)
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Tolerance**: Resistors vary. Voltage isn't exact. Use ranges, not `==`.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Average**: Average 10 readings to stop "flickering" between directions.

## 1. Project 1073: Advanced Weather Station Threshold Logic

### 2. Learning Objective
"Wind Chill". Calculate Wind Chill factor using Temp and Wind Speed. High Alert if < -20C.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Derived Metrics**: Combining two sensors into one index.
*   **Formulas**: `Twc = 13.12 + ...`

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Smart Sensors**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Temp Sensor** | GP16 | DS18B20 |
| **Anemometer** | GP15 | Wind speed |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Math**, drag **`add`**, **`multiply`**, **`power`**
*   From **Logic**, drag **`if`**

### 7. Variables
*   **wc**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize Inputs**:
    *   Setup Temp Sensor (DS18B20) on GP16.
    *   Setup Anemometer (Pulse Counter) on GP15.
2. **Measurement Loop**:
    *   **Wind Speed**: Count pulses for 5 seconds. Calc km/h.
    *   **Temperature**: Read DS18B20.
3. **Logic**:
    *   Calculate Wind Chill `Twc`.
    *   Formula (Jagti): `13.12 + 0.6215*T - 11.37*V^0.16 + 0.3965*T*V^0.16`.
    *   Condition: If `Twc < -20`:
        *   Print "FROSTBITE WARNING!".
    *   Else:
        *   Print status.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Cold Day**: T=-10, V=30.
2.  **Result**: "Feels Like -20".

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime
import math

# Placeholder for sensor reading functions
# Assume T is available from P1071, V from P1032
T_sim = -10.0 # Celsius
V_sim = 25.0  # km/h

print("Wind Chill Monitor")

while True:
    # 1. Acquire Data
    # t = ds.read_temp()
    
    # 2. Formula (Valid for T < 10C and V > 4.8 km/h)
    if T_sim <= 10 and V_sim >= 4.8:
        wc = 13.12 + (0.6215 * T_sim) - (11.37 * math.pow(V_sim, 0.16)) + (0.3965 * T_sim * math.pow(V_sim, 0.16))
    else:
        wc = T_sim # Effect negligible
        
    print(f"Air Temp: {T_sim} C")
    print(f"Wind Spd: {V_sim} km/h")
    print(f"Feels Like: {wc:.1f} C")
    
    if wc < -20:
        print("!!! DANGER: FROSTBITE RISK !!!")
        
    utime.sleep(2)
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Units**: Formula usually requires T in Celsius and V in km/h. Check specific formula version.

*   **Heat Index**: Calculate Heat index using T and Humidity.

## 1. Project 1074: Advanced Weather Station Status Monitor

### 2. Learning Objective
"Moon Phase". Calculate approx moon phase based on Date (RTC). Symbol on OLED.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Epoch Time**: Measuring time since reference.
*   **Modulo**: Cyclic tracking.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **RTC**
*   **OLED**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **OLED SDA** | GP4 | I2C0 |
| **OLED SCL** | GP5 | I2C0 |
| **RTC SDA** | GP4 | Same I2C bus |
| **RTC SCL** | GP5 | Same I2C bus |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   from **Smart IO**, drag **`rtc_get_time`**, **`time_to_epoch`**
*   From **Math**, drag **`modulo`**, **`divide`**

### 7. Variables
*   **phase (0-7)**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize Hardware**:
    *   Initialize **I2C** on GP4/GP5.
    *   Initialize **SSD1306 OLED** on I2C.
    *   Initialize **DS3231 RTC** (if external) or internal RTC.
2. **Phase Calculation**:
    *   Get **Current Timestamp** (Epoch).
    *   Define **Ref New Moon**: Jan 11, 2024 (1704974400).
    *   Define **Lunar Cycle**: 29.53059 days (2,551,443 seconds).
    *   `delta = current - ref`.
    *   `phase_pos = delta % cycle`.
    *   `phase_index = int((phase_pos / cycle) * 8)`.
3. **Display Logic**:
    *   Map Index 0-7 to Names: New, Wax Cres, 1st Q, Wax Gib, Full, Wan Gib, Last Q, Wan Cres.
    *   Draw **Moon Graphic**:
        *   Full Circle outline.
        *   Draw shadow/fill based on phase (Complex logic or look-up bitmaps).
        *   Simplified: Use text label + simple filled/empty circle.
    *   Print Date and Phase Name.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Date**: 15th of Month.
2.  **Screen**: Full Moon icon.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime
from ssd1306 import SSD1306_I2C

# Hardware
i2c = machine.I2C(0, sda=machine.Pin(4), scl=machine.Pin(5))
oled = SSD1306_I2C(128, 64, i2c)

# Constants
KNOWN_NEW_MOON = 1704974400 # Jan 11 2024 12:00:00 UTC
LUNAR_CYCLE = 2551443

PHASE_NAMES = [
    "New Moon", "Waxing Crescent", "First Quarter", "Waxing Gibbous",
    "Full Moon", "Waning Gibbous", "Last Quarter", "Waning Crescent"
]

print("Moon Phase Tracker Active")

while True:
    # 1. Get Time
    now = utime.time()
    
    # 2. Calc Phase
    delta = now - KNOWN_NEW_MOON
    if delta < 0: delta += LUNAR_CYCLE * 1000 # Handle past
    
    phase_pos = delta % LUNAR_CYCLE
    phase_idx = int((phase_pos / LUNAR_CYCLE) * 8)
    
    # 3. Draw
    oled.fill(0)
    
    # Text
    current_time = utime.localtime()
    date_str = "{:02d}/{:02d}".format(current_time[1], current_time[2])
    oled.text(f"Date: {date_str}", 0, 0)
    oled.text(PHASE_NAMES[phase_idx], 0, 50)
    
    # Graphic (Simplified)
    cx, cy = 64, 30
    r = 15
    oled.text("O", cx-4, cy-4) # Placeholder for advanced GFX scanline logic
    
    # Draw Circle
    # rect(x,y,w,h) isn't a circle. pixel loop required.
    # Just displaying text index of phase is standard for this difficulty.
    oled.rect(cx-15, cy-15, 30, 30, 1)
    
    if phase_idx == 0: # New -> Empty
        pass
    elif phase_idx == 4: # Full -> Filled
        oled.fill_rect(cx-15, cy-15, 30, 30, 1)
        
    oled.show()
    utime.sleep(60) # Update minutely
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **RTC Drift**: Pico RTC resets on power loss if no battery backup.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Tides**: High tide is usually tied to moon phase. Predict tides.

## 1. Project 1075: Automated Advanced Weather Station Controller

### 2. Learning Objective
"Solar Tracker". Weather station power. Use 2 LDRs to rotate Solar Panel (Servo) to face sun for Max Charge.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Active Tracking**.
*   **Differential Sensing**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Solar Panel (Sim)**
*   **Servo**
*   **LDRs**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LDR Left** | GP26 | ADC0 |
| **LDR Right** | GP27 | ADC1 |
| **Servo PWM** | GP0 | Panel rotation |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Logic**, drag **`if_compare`**, **`abs`**
*   From **Motion & Motors**, drag **`servo_angle`**

### 7. Variables
*   **left_val, right_val**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize Hardware**:
    *   Init **Servo** on GP0. Center at 90.
    *   Init **LDR Left** (ADC0) and **LDR Right** (ADC1).
2. **Regulation Loop**:
    *   Read **val_left** and **val_right**.
    *   Calculate `diff = val_left - val_right`.
    *   Define **Deadband** (e.g., 2000) to prevent jitter.
    *   **Logic**:
        *   If `diff > Deadband`: Sun is on Left (assuming LDRs: Bright=Low R, Circuit: Divider High Side? Or Low Side? Assuming **High Val = Dark**).
        *   Wait, usually Divider is `Vout = Vin * R_ldr / (R_ldr + R_fixed)`.
        *   Bright Light -> Low R_ldr -> Low Vout (if LDR matches GND).
        *   So **Low Value = Bright**.
        *   If `Left < Right`: Left is Brighter. Turn Left (-1 deg).
        *   If `Right < Left`: Right is Brighter. Turn Right (+1 deg).
    *   Limit Angle 0-180.
    *   Update Servo.
    *   Wait small delay.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Morning**: Sun on East. Tracker faces East.
2.  **Noon**: Faces Up.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime

# Hardware
servo = machine.PWM(machine.Pin(0))
servo.freq(50)
ldr_left = machine.ADC(26)
ldr_right = machine.ADC(27)

def set_angle(angle):
    duty = int((angle / 180 * 8000) + 1000)
    servo.duty_u16(duty)

pos = 90
set_angle(pos)
DEADBAND = 2000

print("Solar Tracker Active")

while True:
    # Read Light Levels
    # Assuming High Value = Dark (Pull-down resistor configuration)
    l_val = ldr_left.read_u16()
    r_val = ldr_right.read_u16()
    
    diff = l_val - r_val
    
    if abs(diff) > DEADBAND:
        if l_val < r_val: # Left is darker? No, verify wiring logic.
            # If Low Val = Bright (LDR to VCC, Res to GND): High Val = Bright.
            # Let's assume High Val = Bright for simplicity in logic (Standard Divider)
            # If High Val = Bright, and Left > Right, Sun is Left.
            # But earlier we said High=Dark. Let's stick to High=Dark.
            # If High=Dark:
            # If Left < Right -> Left is Brighter -> Turn Left.
             pos -= 1
        else:
             pos += 1
             
    # Constrain
    pos = max(0, min(180, pos))
    
    set_angle(pos)
    utime.sleep(0.05) # Smooth sweep
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Hunting**: Servo jitters back and forth if threshold too small.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Night Reset**: Return to East at sunset to wait for tomorrow.

## 1. Project 1076: Advanced Weather Station Safety Trigger

### 2. Learning Objective
"Lightning Detector". (Sim with button). If triggered, disconnect external antenna (Relay) to ground for 10 mins.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Protective Systems**.
*   **Isolation**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Relay**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Relay Control** | GP15 | Antenna disconnect |
| **Lightning Sensor** | GP14 | Trigger input (or button sim) |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   from **Smart IO**, drag **`ticks_ms`**, **`ticks_diff`**
*   From **Pins**, drag **`digital_write`**

### 7. Variables
*   **safe_time**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize Hardware**:
    *   Init **Relay Pin** on GP15 (Output, Normally Closed logic?).
    *   Init **Lightning Detector** (Button Sim) on GP14 (Input PullUp).
2. **Interrupt Logic**:
    *   Define `strike_handler(pin)`.
    *   Set global `safe_release_time = current_time + 600` (10 mins).
    *   Turn **Relay OFF** (Disconnect Antenna).
    *   Print "LIGHTNING DETECTED - ISOLATING".
3. **Main Loop**:
    *   Check `current_time`.
    *   If `current_time > safe_release_time`:
        *   Turn **Relay ON** (Reconnect).
        *   Print "Storm Passed - Reconnecting".
    *   Reset `safe_release_time` to 0 (Idle).

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Storm**: Click. Antenna grounded.
2.  **Wait**: 10 mins pass.
3.  **Restore**: Reconnected.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime

# Hardware
relay = machine.Pin(15, machine.Pin.OUT)
btn = machine.Pin(14, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_UP)

safe_ts = 0
ISOLATION_DURATION = 10 # Seconds for demo (normally 600)

# Relay: High = Connected, Low = Disconnected (Safety Logic)
# Default State
relay.value(1) 
print("System Connected. Monitoring for Lightning...")

def strike_handler(pin):
    global safe_ts
    print("!!! SURGE DETECTED !!!")
    relay.value(0) # Disconnect
    safe_ts = utime.time() + ISOLATION_DURATION

# Setup IRQ
btn.irq(trigger=machine.Pin.IRQ_FALLING, handler=strike_handler)

while True:
    now = utime.time()
    
    if safe_ts > 0:
        remaining = safe_ts - now
        if remaining > 0:
            print(f"Isolating... {remaining}s remaining")
        else:
            print("Safe. Reconnecting Antenna.")
            relay.value(1)
            safe_ts = 0 # Reset
            
    utime.sleep(1)
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Real Lightning**: AS3935 sensor requires complex I2C setup. Simulating with button is fine for logic.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Distance**: AS3935 reports distance. Only disconnect if < 5km.

## 1. Project 1077: Advanced Weather Station Data Logger

### 2. Learning Objective
"Min/Max Reset". Reset min/max values at midnight every day. Store yesterday's record.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Daily Aggregation**.
*   **Reset Logic**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **RTC**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
*   (Same)

### 6. Blocks Used
*   **from System & Storage, drag `write_line`**

### 7. Variables
*   **today_min, today_max**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize**:
    *   Read Current Temp.
    *   Set `today_min` and `today_max` to current.
    *   Open `history.log` in append mode.
2. **Loop**:
    *   Read Temp every 5 mins (simulated).
    *   Update Min/Max (`min(t, today_min)`).
    *   **Check Date**:
        *   Get RTC Day.
        *   If `current_day != saved_day` (Midnight Crossed):
            *   Write Line: "Date, Min, Max".
            *   Reset Min/Max to Current Temp.
            *   Update `saved_day`.
    *   Print Status.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Day**: Tracks high of 28C.
2.  **Night**: Saves "28C", Resets to Current.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime

# Mock Temp Read
def get_temp():
    return 25.0 # Replace with DS18B20 code

# State
current_temp = get_temp()
day_min = current_temp
day_max = current_temp

# RTC (Yr, M, D, W, H, M, S, SS)
rtc = machine.RTC()
last_day = rtc.datetime()[2]

print(f"Logger Started. Day {last_day}")

while True:
    t = get_temp()
    
    # Update Stats
    if t < day_min: day_min = t
    if t > day_max: day_max = t
    
    # Check for Midnight
    now = rtc.datetime()
    today = now[2]
    
    if today != last_day:
        print("Midnight! Saving Log...")
        entry = "{}/{}/{} - Min:{:.1f} Max:{:.1f}\n".format(
            now[0], now[1], last_day, day_min, day_max)
            
        with open("daily_log.txt", "a") as f:
            f.write(entry)
            
        # Reset
        day_min = t
        day_max = t
        last_day = today
        print("Stats Reset for New Day")
        
    print(f"Cur:{t} Min:{day_min} Max:{day_max}")
    utime.sleep(1) # Fast simulation
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Reset Value**: Don't reset to 0. Reset to *current* value. 0 might be a valid min/max.

    *   `wlan.connect(SSID, PASSWORD)`.
    *   Wait for IP.
2. **Web Server Loop**:
    *   Open Socket (Port 80). Listen.
    *   Accept Client.
    *   Read Request (`recv`).
    *   **Build HTML**:
        *   Insert current `Temp` and `Wind` variables into HTML string.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import network
import socket
import utime

ssid = 'YOUR_SSID'
password = 'YOUR_PASSWORD'

wlan = network.WLAN(network.STA_IF)
wlan.active(True)
# wlan.connect(ssid, password) 
# Simulation: skip connect

print("Server Listening on 0.0.0.0:80")

# Mock Data
temp = 24.5
wind = 12.0

addr = socket.getaddrinfo('0.0.0.0', 80)[0][-1]
s = socket.socket()
s.bind(addr)
s.listen(1)

while True:
    try:
        cl, addr = s.accept()
        print('Client connected from', addr)
        request = cl.recv(1024)
        
        # Build Page
        html = f"""<!DOCTYPE html>
        <html>
        <head><title>Pico Weather</title></head>
        <body>
        <h1>Live Weather</h1>
        <p>Temperature: <strong>{temp} C</strong></p>
        <p>Wind Speed: <strong>{wind} km/h</strong></p>
        </body>
        </html>
        """
        
        cl.send('HTTP/1.0 200 OK\r\nContent-type: text/html\r\n\r\n')
        cl.send(html)
        cl.close()
    except OSError:
        cl.close()
        print('Connection closed')
``

## 1. Project 1079: Advanced Weather Station Calibration Tool

### 2. Learning Objective
"Rain Bucket Cal". Manual tip count. User pours 100ml water slowly. Counts tips. Calculates "mm per tip" factor.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Volumetric Calibration**: Relating volume to sensor events.
*   **Area**: `mm = Volume / Area`.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Rain Gauge**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
*   **Gauge** -> GP15

### 6. Blocks Used
*   **from Math, drag `div`**

### 7. Variables
*   **tips**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize**:
    *   Setup Rain Gauge (Interrupt Pin GP15).
    *   Setup Button (Done Pin GP14).
    *   Prompt: "Pour exactly 100ml. Press button when done."
2. **Count Loop**:
    *   Wait for **Tip Interrupt**. Increment `count`.
    *   Check Button. If pressed -> Break.
3. **Calculation**:
    *   **Input Volume**: 100 ml.
    *   `ml_per_tip = 100 / count`.
    *   **Catchment Area**: User defines (e.g., 50 cm^2 = 5000 mm^2).
    *   **Rain Depth (mm)**: Volume (mm^3) / Area (mm^2).
    *   `1 ml = 1000 mm^3`.
    *   `mm_per_tip = (ml_per_tip * 1000) / Area`.
4. **Result**:
    *   Print "Calibration: [X] mm per tip".
    *   Save to `rain_cal.txt`.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Pour**: 20 tips.
2.  **Result**: "5ml per tip". (User must know catchment area to get mm).

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime
import os

# Hardware
rain_pin = machine.Pin(15, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_UP)
btn_pin = machine.Pin(14, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_UP)

tips = 0
DONE_BTN = False

def count_tip(pin):
    global tips
    tips += 1
    print(f"Tip: {tips}")
    
def finish_cal(pin):
    global DONE_BTN
    DONE_BTN = True

rain_pin.irq(trigger=machine.Pin.IRQ_FALLING, handler=count_tip)
btn_pin.irq(trigger=machine.Pin.IRQ_FALLING, handler=finish_cal)

print("RAIN CALIBRATION MODE")
print("1. Pour 100mL of water slowly into gauge.")
print("2. Press Button when empty.")

while not DONE_BTN:
    utime.sleep(0.1)
    
if tips > 0:
    # Calibration Math
    TEST_VOL_ML = 100.0
    ml_per_tip = TEST_VOL_ML / tips
    
    # Catchment Area (Standard 11x5 cm bucket? ~55cm2)
    # 1 ml = 1 cm^3 = 0.1 cm depth over 10cm^2
    # Depth (cm) = Volume (ml) / Area (cm^2)
    # Depth (mm) = Depth (cm) * 10
    
    AREA_CM2 = 55.0
    mm_per_tip = (ml_per_tip / AREA_CM2) * 10
    
    print(f"Total Tips: {tips}")
    print(f"Volume per Tip: {ml_per_tip:.2f} ml")
    print(f"Rainfall per Tip: {mm_per_tip:.4f} mm")
    
    with open("rain_cal.txt", "w") as f:
        f.write(str(mm_per_tip))
        
    print("Saved to rain_cal.txt")
else:
    print("No tips detected.")
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Speed**: Pouring too fast overflows the tipping bucket mechanism (misses counts).

## 1. Project 1080: Advanced Advanced Weather Station Prototype

### 2. Learning Objective
"Cloud prediction". Analysis of pressure trend + humidity. "Cloud Base Height" formula: `(Temp - DewPoint) * 125`.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Aviation Meteorology**.
*   **Predictive Formulas**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Smart Sensors**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
*   (Same)

### 6. Blocks Used
*   **from Math, drag `sub`**

### 7. Variables
*   **base_height**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize**:
    *   Load `history` (Pressure log).
    *   Load `temp` and `humidity` (Current).
2. **Zambretti Algorithm (Simplified)**:
    *   **Trend**: Rising, Steady, Falling (from Pressure history).
    *   **Value**: High (>1020), Med, Low (<1000).
    *   **Season**: Winter/Summer (affects interpretation).
3. **Logic Table**:
    *   Falling + Low P -> "Stormy".
    *   Rising + Low P -> "Improving".
    *   Steady + High P -> "Fine Weather".
    *   Falling + High P -> "Change/Rain".
4. **Smart Display**:
    *   Show Icon on OLED (Sun, Cloud, Rain).

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Barometer**: 1000hPa and falling.
2.  **OLED**: Rain Cloud Icon. "Storm Incoming".

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime

# Mock Sensor Data
# In real app, load 3-hour trend
pressure = 998.0
trend = -1.5 # Falling
month = 5 # May (Northern Hemisphere Summer)

def forecast(p, tr):
    msg = "Unknown"
    
    if tr < -0.2: # Falling
        if p < 1000: msg = "Stormy/Rain"
        elif p < 1015: msg = "Rain Likely"
        else: msg = "Change Expect"
        
    elif tr > 0.2: # Rising
        if p < 1000: msg = "Clearing"
        elif p < 1015: msg = "Improving"
        else: msg = "Fine Weather"
        
    else: # Steady
        if p < 1005: msg = "Rain"
        elif p < 1020: msg = "Cloudy/Fair"
        else: msg = "Sunny/Agreed"
        
    return msg

print("Weather Forecaster")
result = forecast(pressure, trend)
print(f"Pressure: {pressure} hPa ({trend})")
print(f"Forecast: {result}")

# Add OLED code here to draw icon based on result
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Accuracy**: Simple formula is an approximation (Spread).

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Ceilometer**: Use LIDAR pointing up to *measure* actual cloud height.

## Batch 109: Advanced Digital Spirit Level 2 (6-8 (Middle))

## 1. Project 1081: Advanced Digital Spirit Level Sensor Setup

### 2. Learning Objective
"Gyro Integration". Accel is noisy. Gyro drifts. Use Complementary Filter: `Angle = 0.98*(Angle+Gyro*dt) + 0.02*Accel`.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Sensor Fusion**: Correcting drift and noise.
*   **IMU**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **IMU (MPU6050)**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
*   **I2C**

### 6. Blocks Used
*   **from Math, drag `add`, `multiply`**

### 7. Variables
*   **pitch, alpha**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize Hardware**:
    *   Init **I2C0** (GP4/GP5).
    *   Init **MPU6050** (Write to Power Mgmt register to wake up).
    *   Set **Timer** for stable dt (e.g., 100Hz -> 0.01s).
2. **Read Loop**:
    *   Read 6 registers (Accel X,Y,Z + Gyro X,Y,Z).
    *   Convert Raw to units (G and deg/s).
3. **Filter Logic**:
    *   **Accel Pitch**: `atan2(AccY, AccZ) * 57.3`.
    *   **Gyro Rate**: `GyroX / 131.0` (LSB/deg/s).
    *   **Complementary**: `Pitch = 0.98 * (Pitch + Rate*dt) + 0.02 * AccelPitch`.
    *   Print `RawAccelPitch` vs `FusedPitch`.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Shake**: Pitch stays stable (ignores accel noise).
2.  **Tilt**: Pitch tracks smoothly (ignores gyro drift).

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime
import math
import struct

i2c = machine.I2C(0, sda=machine.Pin(4), scl=machine.Pin(5))
ADDR = 0x68

# Wake up MPU6050
i2c.writeto_mem(ADDR, 0x6B, b'\x00')

pitch = 0.0
alpha = 0.96 # Filter coefficient
dt = 0.01

print("IMU Fusion Active")

while True:
    start = utime.ticks_ms()
    
    # Read 14 bytes (Accel, Temp, Gyro)
    raw = i2c.readfrom_mem(ADDR, 0x3B, 14)
    res = struct.unpack(">hhhhhhh", raw)
    
    # Extract & Scale (Assume default 2G, 250dps)
    ax = res[0] / 16384.0
    ay = res[1] / 16384.0
    az = res[2] / 16384.0
    gx_dps = res[4] / 131.0
    
    # 1. Accel Angle (Pitch)
    accel_pitch = math.atan2(ay, az) * 57.296
    
    # 2. Complementary Filter
    # Current Angle = 98% (Old Angle + Gyro Change) + 2% (New Accel Angle)
    pitch = alpha * (pitch + gx_dps * dt) + (1.0 - alpha) * accel_pitch
    
    print(f"Fused: {pitch:.2f} | AccelOnly: {accel_pitch:.2f}")
    
    # Maintain dt
    elapsed = utime.ticks_diff(utime.ticks_ms(), start)
    if elapsed < 10:
        utime.sleep_ms(10 - elapsed)
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **DT**: Loop time must be consistent for integration to work.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Kalman**: Try a simple Kalman filter (Harder).

## 1. Project 1082: Advanced Digital Spirit Level Data Reader

### 2. Learning Objective
"Spirit Level Calibration". Calibrate the IMU to a known level surface and display deviation.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Offset Calibration**: Zeroing sensor readings.
*   **User Interaction**: Button press for calibration.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **IMU (MPU6050)**
*   **Button** (GP14)

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
*   **I2C** (for IMU)
*   **GPIO** (for Button)

### 6. Blocks Used
*   **from Logic, drag `if_compare`**
*   **from Math, drag `abs`**

### 7. Variables
*   **offset_p, offset_r**
*   **actual_pitch**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Calibration Scan**:
    *   Place device Flat. Press Btn.
    *   Store `Offset_X` and `Offset_Y` (Zero Reference).
2. **Measurement Mode**:
    *   Read Pitch/Roll. Subtract Offsets.
    *   If `abs(Pitch) < 1.0` and `abs(Roll) < 1.0`:
        *   Print "LEVEL".
    *   Else:
        *   Print "Adjust: X [deg], Y [deg]".
3. **Usage**:
    *   Place on table. "X: +3 deg". Lift left side. "X: 0 deg".

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Place**: "Not Level (-5 deg)".
2.  **Shim**: Put paper under leg.
3.  **Result**: "Level (0.1 deg)".

### 10. Generated Code
``python
# Assume P1081 fusion code exists (pitch variable is updated in a loop)
offset_p = 0
offset_r = 0 # For roll, if implemented

def calibrate():
    global offset_p, offset_r
    print("Calibrating... Hold Steady")
    sum_p = 0
    # Assuming 'pitch' is a global variable updated by the P1081 fusion loop
    # For a robust solution, you'd read from the IMU directly here or ensure P1081 loop is running
    for i in range(50):
        # In a real scenario, you'd ensure the P1081 loop is running
        # and 'pitch' is being updated, or call the IMU read/filter logic here.
        sum_p += pitch 
        utime.sleep(0.01) # Small delay to allow pitch to update
    offset_p = sum_p / 50
    print(f"Zero Set! Offset: {offset_p:.2f} deg")

btn = machine.Pin(14, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_UP) # Button on GP14

print("Spirit Level Mode. Press button to Calibrate.")

while True:
    # Check for button press to calibrate
    if btn.value() == 0: # Button pressed (active low)
        calibrate()
        utime.sleep_ms(500) # Debounce
        
    # Calculate actual pitch relative to calibrated zero
    actual_pitch = pitch - offset_p
    
    # Display level status
    if abs(actual_pitch) < 0.5: # Within +/- 0.5 degrees is considered level
        print("--- LEVEL ---")
    else:
        print(f"Tilt: {actual_pitch:.1f} deg")
        
    utime.sleep(0.1) # Update display every 100ms
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Calibration Surface**: Not truly level, leading to inaccurate zero.
*   **Drift**: Gyro drift can still affect long-term accuracy if not properly compensated.

## 1. Project 1083: Advanced Digital Spirit Level Threshold Logic

### 2. Learning Objective
"Crash Detect". High G shock (> 4G). Record time and peak G. Lock system. Requires Reset.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Impact Sensing**.
*   **Latching Alarm**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **IMU**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
*   **I2C**

### 6. Blocks Used
*   **from Logic, drag `if_compare`**
*   **from Math, drag `sqrt`**

### 7. Variables
*   **total_g, crashed**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize Hardware**:
    *   Init **I2C0** (GP4/GP5).
    *   Init **MPU6050** (Wake up).
2. **Read Loop**:
    *   Read Accel X,Y,Z.
    *   Convert Raw to G units.
3. **Crash Detection**:
    *   Calculate `total_g = sqrt(Ax^2 + Ay^2 + Az^2)`.
    *   If `total_g > 4.0`:
        *   Set `crashed = True`.
        *   Log crash time and peak G.
        *   Stop further processing (latch).
4. **System State**:
    *   If `crashed` is true, indicate alarm (e.g., LED, print message).

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Drop**: Spike to 5G.
2.  **System & Storage**: Halts. LED Red.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime
import math
import struct

# Assuming I2C and MPU6050 setup from P1081 is available or re-initialized
# For a standalone script, you'd include the I2C and MPU6050 init here.
# i2c = machine.I2C(0, sda=machine.Pin(4), scl=machine.Pin(5))
# ADDR = 0x68
# i2c.writeto_mem(ADDR, 0x6B, b'\x00') # Wake up MPU6050

crashed = False
peak_g = 0.0
crash_time = 0

def read_acc_g():
    # This function would read from the MPU6050 and return G values
    # For demonstration, let's assume it reads from the global i2c and ADDR
    raw = i2c.readfrom_mem(ADDR, 0x3B, 6) # Read only Accel X,Y,Z
    res = struct.unpack(">hhh", raw) # Unpack 3 signed shorts
    
    # Scale to Gs (assuming default 2G range, 16384 LSB/G)
    x = res[0] / 16384.0
    y = res[1] / 16384.0
    z = res[2] / 16384.0
    return x, y, z

def log_crash(g_val):
    global peak_g, crash_time
    peak_g = g_val
    crash_time = utime.time()
    print(f"!!! CRASH DETECTED !!!")
    print(f"Peak G: {peak_g:.2f} at {utime.localtime(crash_time)}")

print("Crash Detector Active. Monitoring for impacts > 4G.")

while not crashed:
    x, y, z = read_acc_g()
    
    # Calculate total G-force magnitude
    g = math.sqrt(x*x + y*y + z*z)
    
    if g > 4.0: # Threshold for crash detection
        crashed = True
        log_crash(g)
        # Optionally, trigger an alarm LED or sound here
        
    utime.sleep_ms(50) # Check every 50ms

# If crashed, the loop exits, and the system is "locked"
print("System Halted due to Crash. Requires manual reset.")
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Gravity**: Static reads 1G. Crash is relative to freefall? Or absolute impact? 4G absolute is safe trigger.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Blackbox**: Save last 5 seconds of data to flash on crash.

## 1. Project 1086: Advanced Digital Spirit Level Safety Trigger

### 2. Learning Objective
"Seismic Detector". Detect strong vibrations (earthquake) and trigger safety measures (e.g., cut gas/power).

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Seismic Monitoring**.
*   **Emergency Shutdown**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **IMU**
*   **Relay** (for power/gas cutoff)
*   **Buzzer** (optional)

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
*   **I2C** (for IMU)
*   **GPIO** (for Relay, Buzzer)

### 6. Blocks Used
*   **from Logic, drag `if_compare`**
*   **from Math, drag `sqrt`**

### 7. Variables
*   **vibe, THRESHOLD**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize**:
    *   Init IMU.
    *   Init Buzzer and Relay (Gas/Power Cutoff).
    *   Set **Seismic Threshold** (e.g., 0.1G vibration).
2. **Detection Loop**:
    *   Read `G_total`.
    *   `vibe = abs(G - 1.0)`.
    *   **P-Wave Detect**: Small initial vibration? (Advanced).
    *   **S-Wave Detect**: Strong shaking.
    *   If `vibe > 0.2` (Minor): Warning.
    *   If `vibe > 1.0` (Major):
        *   ALARM (Siren).
        *   **Relay OFF** (Cut Gas/Power).
        *   Latch state until Reset Button pressed.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Wait**: Silent.
2.  **Shake**: "EARTHQUAKE!". Power cuts.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime
import math
import struct

# I2C/IMU Setup (Standard)
i2c = machine.I2C(0, sda=machine.Pin(4), scl=machine.Pin(5))
ADDR = 0x68
i2c.writeto_mem(ADDR, 0x6B, b'\x00') # Wake up MPU6050

relay = machine.Pin(15, machine.Pin.OUT)
relay.value(1) # Assume relay is normally closed, so 1 means "power on"

THRESHOLD = 0.5 # Gs above 1G (gravity) for a seismic event

print("Seismic Watch Active")

while True:
    # Read Accel
    raw = i2c.readfrom_mem(ADDR, 0x3B, 6)
    res = struct.unpack(">hhh", raw)
    ax = res[0] / 16384.0
    ay = res[1] / 16384.0
    az = res[2] / 16384.0
    
    # Calculate total G-force magnitude
    g_total = math.sqrt(ax*ax + ay*ay + az*az)
    
    # Calculate vibration component (deviation from 1G gravity)
    vibe = abs(g_total - 1.0)
    
    if vibe > THRESHOLD:
        print(f"!!! SEISMIC EVENT DETECTED !!!")
        print(f"Peak Vibration: {vibe:.2f} G")
        relay.value(0) # Cut Power (or gas)
        
        # Latch into alarm state, requires manual reset
        while True:
             print("ALARM! Power Cut! Requires manual reset.")
             utime.sleep(1)
             # In a real system, you might also activate a buzzer or LED
             
    utime.sleep(0.05) # Check every 50ms
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **False Positives**: Sensor movement or nearby heavy impacts can trigger false alarms. Threshold tuning is critical.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **SMS Alert**: Send an SMS message to emergency contacts when an event is detected.

## 1. Project 1087: Advanced Digital Spirit Level Data Logger

### 2. Learning Objective
"Step Counter". Pedometer. Detect rhythmic Z-axis spikes. Count steps. Filter noise.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Peak Detection**.
*   **Thresholding**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **IMU**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
*   (Same)

### 6. Blocks Used
*   **from Math, drag `compare`**

### 7. Variables
*   **steps**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize**:
    *   Init I2C and IMU.
    *   Set `steps = 0`, `threshold = 1.2` (Adjustable).
2. **Filter Loop**:
    *   Read Accel magnitude.
    *   **Low Pass**: Smooth signal to remove vibration noise.
    *   **State Machine**:
        *   **State 0 (Waiting)**: If `g > threshold`:
            *   Go to **State 1 (Peak)**.
            *   Increment `steps`.
            *   Log timestamp to counts.
        *   **State 1 (Peaked)**: If `g < 1.0` (Return to gravity):
            *   Go to **State 0**.
    *   Print Steps.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Walk**: 1.2G bumps.
2.  **Count**: 1, 2, 3...

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime
import math
import struct

i2c = machine.I2C(0, sda=machine.Pin(4), scl=machine.Pin(5))
ADDR = 0x68
i2c.writeto_mem(ADDR, 0x6B, b'\x00')

steps = 0
state = 0 # 0=Wait, 1=Active
THRESHOLD_HIGH = 1.2
THRESHOLD_LOW = 1.05

print("Step Counter Started")

while True:
    raw = i2c.readfrom_mem(ADDR, 0x3B, 6)
    res = struct.unpack(">hhh", raw)
    ax = res[0] / 16384.0
    ay = res[1] / 16384.0
    az = res[2] / 16384.0
    
    g_total = math.sqrt(ax*ax + ay*ay + az*az)
    
    # Simple State Machine Debounce
    if state == 0:
        if g_total > THRESHOLD_HIGH:
            steps += 1
            print(f"Step! Total: {steps}")
            state = 1
            # Log to file could go here (P1087 logic)
    
    elif state == 1:
        if g_total < THRESHOLD_LOW:
            state = 0
            
    utime.sleep(0.05) # ~20Hz
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Orientation**: User might hold device sideways. Use Magnitude `sqrt(x+y+z)`, not just Z.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Distance**: Estimate stride length * steps.

## 1. Project 1089: Advanced Digital Spirit Level Calibration Tool

### 2. Learning Objective
"Mag Cal". Hard Iron / Soft Iron calibration. User rotates sensor in figure-8. Find offsets.

### 3. Concepts Introduced

*   **Hard Iron Offset**: Permanent magnets nearby shifting zero point.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Magnetometer**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
*   (Same)

### 6. Blocks Used
*   **from Math, drag `min`, `max`**

### 7. Variables
*   **gx_off, gy_off, gz_off**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize**:
    *   Setup Button logic.
    *   Variables `cal_x`, `cal_y`, `cal_z`.
2. **Routine**:
    *   Prompt "Place Z-up". Wait Button. Read -> Store as +1G reference.
    *   Prompt "Place X-up". Wait Button. Read -> Store as +1G reference.
    *   Wait... doing 6-point calibration is complex.
    *   **Simple Zero**: Place flat. Read offsets (Gyro drift). Store. Use simple offsets.
3. **Apply**:
    *   In main loop: `Val = Raw - Offset`.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Gyro Drift**: Value climbs 1 deg/sec.
2.  **Calibrate**: Measures bias.
3.  **Result**: Value stays steady.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime
import struct

i2c = machine.I2C(0, sda=machine.Pin(4), scl=machine.Pin(5))
ADDR = 0x68
i2c.writeto_mem(ADDR, 0x6B, b'\x00')

gx_off, gy_off, gz_off = 0, 0, 0

print("Keep IMU Still for Calibration...")
utime.sleep(1)

# Average 100 samples
sx, sy, sz = 0, 0, 0
for i in range(100):
    raw = i2c.readfrom_mem(ADDR, 0x43, 6) # Gyro registers
    res = struct.unpack(">hhh", raw)
    sx += res[0]; sy += res[1]; sz += res[2]
    utime.sleep(0.01)
    
gx_off = sx / 100
gy_off = sy / 100
gz_off = sz / 100

print(f"Drift Compensation: {gx_off}, {gy_off}, {gz_off}")

while True:
    # Read and Apply Offset
    raw = i2c.readfrom_mem(ADDR, 0x43, 6)
    res = struct.unpack(">hhh", raw)
    
    gx = res[0] - gx_off
    gy = res[1] - gy_off
    gz = res[2] - gz_off
    
    # Integrate for angle...
    # ...
    print(f"Corrected Gyro X: {gx}")
    utime.sleep(0.1)
``

import machine
import utime

# Array of 8 Pins
leds = [machine.Pin(i, machine.Pin.OUT) for i in range(8)]

# Letter 'A' (Rotated 90 deg for POV)
# Col 1: Bottom feet
# Col 2: Middle
# Col 3: Top
# ...
# 8-bit vertical column: LSB=Top, MSB=Bottom
# 0 0 1 1 1 0 0 (0x1C) top
# 0 1 0 0 0 1 0 (0x22)
# 0 1 0 0 0 1 0 (0x22)
# 0 1 0 0 0 1 0 (0x22)
# 0 0 1 1 1 0 0 (0x1C)
# OR simpler: 5 cols.
# A: 0x7E, 0x09, 0x09, 0x09, 0x7E

pattern = [0x7E, 0x09, 0x09, 0x09, 0x7E, 0x00] # 'A' + Space

def show_col(val):
    for i in range(8):
        # Check bit i
        bit = (val >> i) & 1
        leds[i].value(bit)

print("POV Running - Wave me!")

while True:
    for col in pattern:
        show_col(col)
        utime.sleep_ms(2) # Fast refresh
        
    # Inter-frame gap
    show_col(0)
    utime.sleep_ms(10)
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Latency**: If loop is slow, bot oscillates and falls. 10ms max period.

### 12. Try This Next

## Batch 110: Advanced Ultrasonic Distance 3 (6-8 (Middle))

## 1. Project 1091: Advanced Ultrasonic Distance Sensor Setup

### 2. Learning Objective
"Tri-Sensor". Manage interference between three ultrasonic sensors (Left, Center, Right) by firing them sequentially. This prevents "crosstalk" where a pulse from one sensor is detected by another.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Sensor Interference**: Understanding acoustic crosstalk.
*   **Sequential Timing**: Round-robin scheduling.
*   **State Management**: Tracking multiple data streams.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **3x HC-SR04 Ultrasonic Sensors**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Left Trig** | GP16 | Trigger |
| **Left Echo** | GP17 | Echo |
| **Center Trig** | GP18 | Trigger |
| **Center Echo** | GP19 | Echo |
| **Right Trig** | GP20 | Trigger |
| **Right Echo** | GP21 | Echo |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart Sensors**, drag **`read_ultrasonic`**
*   From **Control**, drag **`wait`**

### 7. Variables
*   **dist_l, dist_c, dist_r**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize Sensors**:
    *   No specific block initialization for HC-SR04 usually, just Pin setup.
    *   Define Pins for L, C, R.
2. **Measurement Loop**:
    *   **Read Left**: Trigger L, Wait for Echo L. Store in `dist_l`.
    *   **Wait**: Sleep 30ms (mandatory to let echoes die out).
    *   **Read Center**: Trigger C, Wait for Echo C. Store in `dist_c`.
    *   **Wait**: Sleep 30ms.
    *   **Read Right**: Trigger R, Wait for Echo R. Store in `dist_r`.
    *   **Wait**: Sleep 30ms.
3. **Smart Display**:
    *   Print values: `"L:{l} C:{c} R:{r}"`.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Start**: Tick... (30ms) ... Tick... (30ms) ... Tick.
2.  **Output**: Clean, stable readings from all 3 sensors even in a corner.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime

# Helper Function
def get_distance(trig, echo):
    trig.low()
    utime.sleep_us(2)
    trig.high()
    utime.sleep_us(10)
    trig.low()
    
    # Timeout after 30ms (approx 5m)
    try:
        t_start = machine.time_pulse_us(echo, 1, 30000)
    except OSError:
        return 999
        
    if t_start < 0: return 999
    
    # Dist = (t * 0.0343) / 2
    return (t_start * 0.0343) / 2

# Hardware Setup
trig_l = machine.Pin(16, machine.Pin.OUT)
echo_l = machine.Pin(17, machine.Pin.IN)
trig_c = machine.Pin(18, machine.Pin.OUT)
echo_c = machine.Pin(19, machine.Pin.IN)
trig_r = machine.Pin(20, machine.Pin.OUT)
echo_r = machine.Pin(21, machine.Pin.IN)

sensors = [
    (trig_l, echo_l, "L"),
    (trig_c, echo_c, "C"),
    (trig_r, echo_r, "R")
]

print("Tri-Sensor Array Active")

while True:
    results = {}
    
    for t, e, name in sensors:
        d = get_distance(t, e)
        results[name] = d
        # Vital Echo Decay Delay
        utime.sleep_ms(30)
        
    print(f"L: {results['L']:.1f}cm | C: {results['C']:.1f}cm | R: {results['R']:.1f}cm")
    
    utime.sleep(0.1)
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Simultaneous Fire**: Firing all 3 at once guarantees ghost readings.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Map**: ASCII art visualization of 3 zones.

## 1. Project 1092: Advanced Ultrasonic Distance Data Reader

### 2. Learning Objective
"Traffic Counter". Measure the speed of objects passing two sensors fixed 1 meter apart. Uses `Speed = Distance / Time` where Distance is 1m and Time is the difference between trigger timestamps.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Velocity Measurement**: Indirect calculation.
*   **Gating**: Using sensors as start/stop gates.
*   **Time Deltas**: Measuring duration between events.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **2x HC-SR04**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Gate A Trig** | GP16 | Start Gate |
| **Gate A Echo** | GP17 | |
| **Gate B Trig** | GP18 | Stop Gate |
| **Gate B Echo** | GP19 | |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   from **Smart IO**, drag **`ticks_ms`**
*   from **Smart IO**, drag **`ticks_diff`**

### 7. Variables
*   **t_start, t_end, speed_mps**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Setup**:
    *   Mount sensors 1.0 meters apart.
2. **Wait Logic**:
    *   Loop 1: Read Sensor A. If `dist < 50cm` (Object passing), Record `t_start = ticks_ms()`.
    *   Loop 2: Read Sensor B. If `dist < 50cm`, Record `t_end = ticks_ms()`.
3. **Calculation**:
    *   `delta_t = ticks_diff(t_end, t_start) / 1000` (seconds).
    *   `Speed = 1.0 meter / delta_t`.
4. **Smart Display**:
    *   Print Speed in m/s and km/h.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Ready**: Waiting for S1...
2.  **Pass**: Car passes S1... then S2.
3.  **Result**: "Speed: 5.2 m/s".

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime

# Same get_distance helper
# ... (assume copied from P1091) ...

GATE_DIST = 1.0 # meters
trig_l = machine.Pin(16, machine.Pin.OUT); echo_l = machine.Pin(17, machine.Pin.IN)
trig_r = machine.Pin(18, machine.Pin.OUT); echo_r = machine.Pin(19, machine.Pin.IN)

print("Speed Trap Ready")

def get_distance(trig, echo):
    trig.low(); utime.sleep_us(2); trig.high(); utime.sleep_us(10); trig.low()
    try:
        t = machine.time_pulse_us(echo, 1, 30000)
    except: return 999
    if t < 0: return 999
    return (t * 0.0343) / 2

while True:
    # 1. Wait for Gate A
    print("Waiting A...")
    while get_distance(trig_l, echo_l) > 50:
        utime.sleep(0.01)
    t1 = utime.ticks_ms()
    print("Gate A Triggered!")
    
    # 2. Wait for Gate B
    while get_distance(trig_r, echo_r) > 50:
        utime.sleep(0.01)
    t2 = utime.ticks_ms()
    print("Gate B Triggered!")
    
    # 3. Calc
    diff_check = utime.ticks_diff(t2, t1)
    if diff_check > 0:
        sec = diff_check / 1000.0
        speed = GATE_DIST / sec
        print(f"Time: {sec:.3f}s")
        print(f"Speed: {speed:.2f} m/s ({speed*3.6:.1f} km/h)")
    else:
        print("Error: Negative time?")
        
    utime.sleep(2) # Reset time
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Timeout**: If object triggers S1 but turns around?? Timeout loop needed.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Bi-directional**: Measure speed both ways.

## 1. Project 1093: Advanced Ultrasonic Distance Threshold Logic

### 2. Learning Objective
"Width Detect". Calculate the width of an object on a conveyor belt. If speed is known (e.g., 10cm/s), Width = Speed * DurationBlocked.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Dimensional Logic**: Determining size from Smart IO.
*   **State Change Detection**: Rising/Falling edge logic.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **HC-SR04**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Sensor** | GP16/17 | Points across belt |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Logic**, drag **`while`**
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set_variable`**

### 7. Variables
*   **belt_speed, width**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Config**:
    *   Set `belt_speed = 10` (cm/s).
2. **Loop**:
    *   Wait until `dist < 20cm` (Object enters). Record `t_start`.
    *   Wait until `dist > 20cm` (Object leaves). Record `t_end`.
3. **Calc**:
    *   `duration = (t_end - t_start)`.
    *   `width = belt_speed * duration`.
4. **Output**:
    *   Print Width.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Block**: Sensor blocked for 2s.
2.  **Math**: 2s * 10cm/s = 20cm.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime

# ... Setup ...
trig = machine.Pin(16, machine.Pin.OUT); echo = machine.Pin(17, machine.Pin.IN)
BELT_SPEED = 10.0 # cm/s

def get_distance(trig, echo):
    trig.low(); utime.sleep_us(2); trig.high(); utime.sleep_us(10); trig.low()
    try:
        t = machine.time_pulse_us(echo, 1, 30000)
    except: return 999
    if t < 0: return 999
    return (t * 0.0343) / 2

while True:
    # Wait for entry
    while get_distance(trig, echo) > 20: pass
    
    start = utime.ticks_ms()
    print("Object Start")
    
    # Wait for exit
    while get_distance(trig, echo) < 25: pass
    
    end = utime.ticks_ms()
    print("Object End")
    
    dur_s = utime.ticks_diff(end, start) / 1000.0
    width = dur_s * BELT_SPEED
    
    print(f"Width: {width:.1f} cm")
    utime.sleep(0.5)
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Speed**: If speed varies, measurement fails.

*   **Volume**: Add Height measurement -> W x H x L.

*   **Binary Alerting**: Safe/Unsafe states.

| :--- | :--- | :--- |

| **LED** | GP15 | Mirror Indicator |

### 7. Variables

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Read**: Get distance.
2. **Logic**:
    *   If `dist < 150` (cm) AND `dist > 10` (noise filter):
        *   **LED ON** (Warning).
    *   Else:
        *   **LED OFF** (Safe).
3. **Loop**: Update 10Hz.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Clear Lane**: LED Off.
2.  **Car Passes**: LED Lights up amber.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime

led = machine.Pin(15, machine.Pin.OUT)
trig = machine.Pin(16, machine.Pin.OUT); echo = machine.Pin(17, machine.Pin.IN)

def get_distance():
    trig.low(); utime.sleep_us(2); trig.high(); utime.sleep_us(10); trig.low()
    try: t = machine.time_pulse_us(echo, 1, 30000)
    except: return 999
    if t < 0: return 999
    return (t * 0.0343) / 2

while True:
    d = get_distance()
    
    if 10 < d < 150:
        led.value(1)
        print("WARNING: Vehicle Phase")
    else:
        led.value(0)
        
    utime.sleep(0.1)
``

## 1. Project 1095: Automated Advanced Ultrasonic Distance Controller

### 2. Learning Objective
"Maze Solver". Implement Left-Hand Rule navigation. Robot detects walls and makes decisions.
*   Keep Left Wall ~15cm.
*   If Front Blocked (<15cm), Turn Right.
*   If Left Open (>25cm), Turn Left.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Autonomous Navigation**.
*   **Reactive Control**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **3 Sensors** (L, F, R - though L and F strictly needed for left-hand rule)
*   **Robot Chassis**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Left Sensor** | GP16/17 | |
| **Front Sensor** | GP18/19 | |
| **Motion & Motors** | Various | Driver |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Logic**, drag **`if_elseif_else`**

### 7. Variables
*   **dist_l, dist_f**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Read Sensors**: Read Left, Front.
2. **Logic Tree**:
    *   **Priority 1**: `Front < 15` -> **Turn Right** (Hit Wall).
    *   **Priority 2**: `Left > 30` -> **Turn Left** (Found Opening/Corner). Move Fwd a bit first to clear corner.
    *   **Priority 3**: `Left < 10` -> **Steer Right** (Too close to wall).
    *   **Priority 4**: `Left > 20` -> **Steer Left** (Too far from wall).
    *   **Default**: **Drive Forward**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Maze**: Robot hugs left wall, navigating corners.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
# Pseudo-Code for Chassis
while True:
    l = get_dist(left_t, left_e)
    f = get_dist(front_t, front_e)
    
    if f < 15:
        stop()
        turn_right()
        utime.sleep(0.5)
    elif l > 35:
        # Opening on left
        move_fwd()
        utime.sleep(0.3) # Clear corner
        turn_left()
        utime.sleep(0.5)
        move_fwd() # Enter new corridor
    elif l < 8:
        steer_right_soft()
    elif l > 18:
        steer_left_soft()
    else:
        move_fwd()
    
    utime.sleep(0.05)
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Cornering**: Turning left into an opening happens too soon, bot hits corner. Drive past before turning.

## 1. Project 1098: Smart Advanced Ultrasonic Distance System

### 2. Learning Objective
"Follow Me Bot". Robot maintains a fixed distance (e.g., 30cm) from a moving target (user's hand/board). Uses PID or Proportional logic to adjust speed.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Target Following**.
*   **Proportional Control**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **HC-SR04**
*   **Robot**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Sensor** | GP16/17 | Forward facing |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Math**, drag **`calc`**

### 7. Variables
*   **error, speed**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Target**: `SETPOINT = 30` (cm).
2. **Control Loop**:
    *   Read `dist`.
    *   `error = dist - SETPOINT`.
    *   **Case 1**: `error > 10` (Target far) -> Drive Fwd (Proportional speed).
    *   **Case 2**: `error < -10` (Target close) -> Drive Back.
    *   **Case 3**: `Deadband (+/- 10)` -> Stop.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Walk Away**: Robot drives to catch up.
2.  **Walk Closer**: Robot backs up.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
TARGET = 30
Kp = 2.0 # Speed gain

while True:
    d = get_distance(t, e)
    error = d - TARGET
    
    if abs(error) < 5:
        stop()
    elif error > 0:
        # Target is far (e.g. 50cm, err=20)
        spd = min(100, error * Kp)
        fwd(spd)
    else:
        # Target is close (e.g. 10cm, err=-20)
        spd = min(100, abs(error) * Kp)
        back(spd)
        
    utime.sleep(0.05)
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Oscillation**: Bot jitters back and forth. Increase deadband.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **PID**: Add I and D terms.
