## 1. Project 1100: Advanced Advanced Ultrasonic Distance Prototype

### 2. Learning Objective
"Gesture Air". A vertical menu scroller. Hand height controls selection.
*   10-15cm: Select Item 1.
*   15-20cm: Select Item 2.
*   20-25cm: Select Item 3.
*   Hold for 2s: Enter/Click.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Spatial UI**.
*   **Hysteresis** (prevent flickering between items).

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **HC-SR04**
*   **OLED Display**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Sensor** | GP16/17 | |
| **OLED** | I2C0 | Display Menu |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Logic**, drag **`if_elseif`**
*   From **Smart Display**, drag **`show_text`**

### 7. Variables
*   **selection, timer**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Define Zones**:
    *   Zone A: 5-15cm.
    *   Zone B: 15-25cm.
    *   Zone C: 25-35cm.
2. **Loop**:
    *   Read `dist`.
    *   Determine Zone. Highlight Menu Item.
    *   **Click Check**: If Zone stays same for 2s -> Action.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Hover Low**: "Option 1" highlighted.
2.  **Lift Hand**: "Option 2" highlighted.
3.  **Hold**: "Option 2 Selected!".

### 10. Generated Code
``python
# ... OLED Setup ...
from machine import Pin, I2C
# (OLED lib usually needed here)

items = ["Play", "Settings", "Exit"]
select = 0
hold_time = 0

trig = machine.Pin(16, machine.Pin.OUT); echo = machine.Pin(17, machine.Pin.IN)

def get_dist():
    # standard hcsr04
    trig.low(); utime.sleep_us(2); trig.high(); utime.sleep_us(10); trig.low()
    try: t = machine.time_pulse_us(echo, 1, 30000); return (t*0.0343)/2
    except: return 99

while True:
    d = get_dist()
    
    new_sel = select
    if 5 < d < 15: new_sel = 0
    elif 15 < d < 25: new_sel = 1
    elif 25 < d < 35: new_sel = 2
    
    # Draw Menu (simulated print)
    for i, item in enumerate(items):
        prefix = "> " if i == new_sel else "  "
        print(prefix + item)
    print("---")
        
    # Hold Logic
    if new_sel == select:
        hold_time += 1
        if hold_time > 20: # 2s at 10Hz
            print("CLICKED!")
    else:
        select = new_sel
        hold_time = 0
        
    utime.sleep(0.1)
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Jitter**: Hand shakes. Needs threshold (e.g. +5cm movement).

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Hold**: Hover at 10cm to Select?

### 2. Learning Objective
"POV Display with Custom Font". Implement a custom font (e.g., 5x7 pixels) to display arbitrary text on the POV wand.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Custom Fonts**: Representing characters as pixel data.
*   **Text Rendering**: Iterating through characters and their pixel columns.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **8 LEDs**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LED 0-7** | GP0-GP7 | Vertical LED strip |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Lists**, drag **`get_item`**
*   From **Loops**, drag **`for_loop`**

### 7. Variables
*   **font, msg**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Define Font**:
    *   Create a dictionary where keys are characters and values are lists of bytes (columns).
    *   Each byte represents a vertical column of 8 pixels.
    *   Example: `{'H': [0x1F, 0x04, 0x1F]}` (5x7 font, 3 columns wide).
2. **Input**:
    *   Define the string `msg` to be displayed (e.g., "HELLO").
3. **Render Loop**:
    *   For each character in the `msg` string:
        *   Retrieve its byte pattern from the `font` dictionary.
        *   For each column byte in the character's pattern:
            *   Call `show_col(byte)` (from P1090) to display the column.
            *   Add a small `utime.sleep_ms()` for POV effect.
        *   Add a blank column (`0x00`) and a small delay for spacing between characters.
4. **Smart IO**:
    *   The `sleep_ms` values are critical for the POV effect. Adjust them to match the desired waving speed.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Wave**: "HELLO" floats in the dark.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime

# Array of 8 Pins (from P1090)
leds = [machine.Pin(i, machine.Pin.OUT) for i in range(8)]

def show_col(val):
    for i in range(8):
        bit = (val >> i) & 1
        leds[i].value(bit)

# Simplified Font (5x7 pixels, 8-bit vertical columns)
# Each byte represents a column. LSB is top pixel, MSB is bottom pixel.
# Example 'H':
# 11111 (0x1F)
# 00100 (0x04)
# 11111 (0x1F)
font = {
    'H': [0x1F, 0x04, 0x1F],
    'E': [0x1F, 0x15, 0x15], # 11111, 10101, 10101
    'L': [0x1F, 0x10, 0x10], # 11111, 10000, 10000
    'O': [0x0E, 0x11, 0x0E], # 01110, 10001, 01110
    ' ': [0x00, 0x00] # Two blank columns for space
}

msg = "HELLO"

print("POV with Custom Font Running - Wave me!")

while True:
    for char in msg:
        if char in font:
            cols = font[char]
            for c in cols:
                show_col(c) # Reuse function from P1090
                utime.sleep_ms(2) # Fast refresh for POV
            show_col(0) # Blank column for spacing between letters
            utime.sleep_ms(2) # Gap between characters
            
    show_col(0) # Clear LEDs after displaying the whole message
    utime.sleep_ms(20) # Word gap before repeating the message
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Font Design**: Incorrect bit patterns for characters will result in garbled text.
*   **Smart IO**: Inconsistent `sleep_ms` values can distort the text when waving.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Scrolling Text**: Implement logic to scroll a longer message across the wand.

## 1. Project 1094: Advanced Ultrasonic Distance Status Monitor

### 2. Learning Objective
"Blind Spot". LED in mirror (Sim). Lights up if object in 45-deg rear sector < 2m.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **ADAS** (Advanced Driver Assistance Systems).
*   **Sector Monitoring**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **HC-SR04**
*   **LED**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
*   **LED** -> GP15

### 6. Blocks Used
*   **from Logic, drag `if`**

### 7. Variables
*   **warning**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize**:
    *   Setup Servo and Sonar.
    *   Create **scan_data** list (size 181, init -1).
2. **Scan Sweep**:
    *   Move Servo 0 -> 180.
    *   At each degree, measure distance. Store in list.
3. **Clustering**:
    *   Iterate through list.
    *   If consecutive points have valid distance (e.g., < 50cm) and valid neighbor:
        *   Group them into a **Cluster**.
    *   Calculate **Angular Width** = End Angle - Start Angle.
4. **Classification**:
    *   If Width > 30 degrees: Print "LARGE OBSTACLE".
    *   If Width < 5 degrees: Print "Noise/Pole".
    *   Else: Print "User/Target".

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Scan**: Servo sweeps.
2.  **Result**: "Found Object: Wall (Width 60 deg)".

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime

# Hardware Setup (omitted common setup for brevity in prompt, assume Servo/HCSR04 class)
# ... Include Servo and HCSR04 classes or setup code ...

servo_pin = machine.Pin(0)
pwm = machine.PWM(servo_pin)
pwm.freq(50)
trig = machine.Pin(2, machine.Pin.OUT)
echo = machine.Pin(3, machine.Pin.IN)

def get_dist():
    # ... Standard Sonar Code ...
    trig.low(); utime.sleep_us(2); trig.high(); utime.sleep_us(5); trig.low()
    try:
        t = machine.time_pulse_us(echo, 1, 30000)
        if t < 0: return 200
        return (t / 2) / 29.1
    except: return 200

def set_angle(angle):
    duty = int((angle / 180 * 8000) + 1000)
    pwm.duty_u16(duty)

print("Size Discriminator Scanning...")

while True:
    # 1. Capture Sweep
    scan_data = []
    print("Sweeping...")
    for ang in range(0, 181, 2): # Step 2 deg
        set_angle(ang)
        utime.sleep(0.02)
        d = get_dist()
        if d < 50: # Valid Range
            scan_data.append(1) # Hit
        else:
            scan_data.append(0) # Miss
            
    # 2. Analyze Clusters
    in_object = False
    start_ang = 0
    
    for i, hit in enumerate(scan_data):
        current_angle = i * 2
        
        if hit and not in_object:
            in_object = True
            start_ang = current_angle
            
        elif not hit and in_object:
            in_object = False
            width = current_angle - start_ang
            # filter noise
            if width > 4:
                obj_type = "Wall" if width > 40 else "Person"
                print(f"Object Detected: {obj_type} (Width: {width} deg)")
                
    utime.sleep(1)
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Clutter**: Road noise / ground reflections. Min threshold needed.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Beeper**: Audio warning if Turn Signal is ON + Blind Spot active.

## 1. Project 1095: Automated Advanced Ultrasonic Distance Controller

### 2. Learning Objective
"Maze Solver". Left-Hand Rule. Keep Left distance ~15cm. If Front < 15, Turn Right. If Left > 20, Turn Left.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Autonomous Navigation Logic**.
*   **Wall Following**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **3 Sensors**
### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize**:
    *   Setup Button (GP14).
    *   Setup POV LEDs (GP0-GP7).
    *   Define Messages: `["HELLO", "WORLD", "PICO", "GAME"]`.
    *   Set `index = 0`.
2. **Control Loop**:
    *   Check Button:
        *   If Pressed -> `index = (index + 1) % 4`.
        *   Blink all LEDs to confirm change.
    *   **Smart Display**:
        *   Get `current_msg = msgs[index]`.
        *   Call `draw_text(current_msg)` (Only runs if swinging, if combined with P1091 logic, or valid swing).
    *   `sleep(20ms)`.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **User**: Waves -> "HELLO".
2.  **Click**: Waves -> "WORLD".
3.  **Click**: Waves -> "PICO".

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime

# Hardware
btn = machine.Pin(14, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_UP)
leds = [machine.Pin(i, machine.Pin.OUT) for i in range(8)]

# Font & Helper from P1091/P1092
# ... (Assume show_col and font dict present) ...
font = {'A': [0x7E, 0x09, 0x09, 0x09, 0x7E], 'B': [0x7F, 0x49, 0x49, 0x49, 0x36], ' ': [0x00]}
def show_col(v):
    for i in range(8): leds[i].value((v>>i)&1)

messages = ["AAA", "BBB", "   "]
current_idx = 0

print("Interactive POV Ready")

while True:
    if btn.value() == 0:
         current_idx = (current_idx + 1) % len(messages)
         print(f"Selected: {messages[current_idx]}")
         # Blink Confirm
         show_col(0xFF)
         utime.sleep(0.2)
         show_col(0x00)
         utime.sleep(0.2) # Debounce
         
    # Display Routine (Blocking or Non-Blocking?)
    # For POV, usually need tight loop. 
    # Here we perform one full render cycle per loop iteration
    msg = messages[current_idx]
    
    # Simple Render (Always On)
    for char in msg:
        for col in font.get(char, font[' ']):
            show_col(col)
            utime.sleep_ms(2)
        show_col(0)
        utime.sleep_ms(2)
    
    show_col(0)
    utime.sleep_ms(15) 
``

## 1. Project 1098: Smart Advanced Ultrasonic Distance System

### 2. Learning Objective
"Follow Me". Robot follows a beacon (holding a flat board). Uses PID to maintain angle and distance.
(Simplified: Keep distance 50cm. Angle needs 2 sensors or scanning).

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Human-Robot Interaction (HRI)**.
*   **Station Keeping**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Smart Sensors**
*   **Robot**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
*   (Same)

### 6. Blocks Used
*   **from Motion & Motors, drag `speed`**

### 7. Variables
*   **error**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize**:
    *   Setup Scanner HW.
    *   Init **Robot Position** (x=0, y=0, theta=0).
    *   Init **Map** (List of points).
2. **Move & Scan Loop**:
    *   **Move**: Drive forward 10cm. Update pos (dead reckoning).
    *   **Scan**: Sweep 180 deg.
    *   **Map Update**:
        *   For each scan point (r, alpha):
        *   Global Angle = theta + alpha.
        *   Feature X = x + r * cos(Global Angle).
        *   Feature Y = y + r * sin(Global Angle).
        *   Add (Feature X, Feature Y) to Map List.
    *   **Print**: List of wall points.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Start**: Robot at (0,0). Scans wall ahead at y=50.
2.  **Move**: Robot at (0,10). Scans wall ahead (now dist 40).
3.  **Map**: Features remain effectively at Global Y=50.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime
import math

# Mock Motor/Servo/Sonar functions
# ...

robot_x = 0
robot_y = 0
robot_heading = 0 # Radians (0 = Facing North/Up)

scan_points = [] # Global Map

def scan_sector():
    # Sweep -45 to +45 relative to heading
    for rel_ang_deg in range(-45, 46, 15):
        # Move Head
        servo_ang = 90 + rel_ang_deg
        # servo.move(servo_ang)
        utime.sleep(0.1)
        
        # Measure
        dist = 50 # Mock reading
        
        # Local to Global Transform
        # Total Angle = Heading + Scan Angle
        alpha = math.radians(rel_ang_deg)
        total_theta = robot_heading + alpha
        
        feat_x = robot_x + (dist * math.sin(total_theta))
        feat_y = robot_y + (dist * math.cos(total_theta))
        
        scan_points.append((feat_x, feat_y))
        
def drive_forward(cm):
    global robot_x, robot_y
    # Move
    # motors_go(...)
    utime.sleep(1)
    
    # Update Odometry (Dead Reckoning)
    robot_x += cm * math.sin(robot_heading)
    robot_y += cm * math.cos(robot_heading)
    
# Main SLAM Logic
while True:
    print(f"Pos: ({robot_x:.1f}, {robot_y:.1f})")
    scan_sector()
    print(f"Map Points: {len(scan_points)}")
    
    drive_forward(10)
    utime.sleep(1)
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Lost**: If target moves out of cone, robot stops or hits full speed. Failsafe "If > 200cm, Stop" needed.

## 1. Project 1099: Advanced POV Calibration Tool

### 2. Learning Objective
"Timing Tuner". POV displays require precise timing to match the user's swing speed. This tool allows the user to adjust the `delay_per_pixel` using a potentiometer to find the perfect speed where the image is not stretched or squashed.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Variable Timing**: Adjusting loop delays dynamically.
*   **Calibration UI**: Using inputs to tune parameters.
*   **Visual Feedback**: Direct observation of tuning results.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **8 LEDs**
*   **Potentiometer**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LED 0-7** | GP0-GP7 | POV Strip |
| **Potentiometer** | GP26 | ADC0 |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Sensing**, drag **`read_potentiometer`**
*   From **Math**, drag **`map`**

### 7. Variables
*   **delay_us**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize**:
    *   Setup LEDs (GP0-GP7).
    *   Setup Potentiometer (ADC0).
2. **Calibration Loop**:
    *   Read Potentiometer value (0-65535).
    *   Map to Delay Range: 100us (Fast) to 10000us (Slow).
    *   **Render Pattern**: Display a square or grid pattern (e.g., "BOX").
    *   Use the mapped `delay_us` for the pixel hold time.
3. **Usage**:
    *   Wave the wand.
    *   If image is **Compressed** (thin), **Decrease Speed** (or Increase Delay?). Wait, if compressed, it means pixels are too brief? No, if thin, need to hold pixels longer?
    *   Let's analyze: distance = speed * time. If time is short, width is small. So to widen, increase Time.
    *   Adjust Pot until the "BOX" looks square.
    *   Print the Calibrated `delay_us` value to Console for use in other projects.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Start**: "BOX" looks like "|||". (Too fast).
2.  **Adjust**: Turn knob right.
3.  **Result**: "BOX" looks like a square. Console: "Calibrated Delay: 2500 us".

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime

# Hardware
pot = machine.ADC(26)
leds = [machine.Pin(i, machine.Pin.OUT) for i in range(8)]

# Test Pattern: "X"
# X is 5 wide. 
# 10001
# 01010
# 00100
# 01010
# 10001
font_x = [0x11, 0x0A, 0x04, 0x0A, 0x11]

def show_col(v):
    for i in range(8): leds[i].value((v>>i)&1)

print("POV Calibration Tool")
print("Adjust Potentiometer until 'X' is square.")

while True:
    # Read Pot -> Delay (range 100us to 5000us)
    # 65535 -> 5000
    reading = pot.read_u16()
    delay_us = 100 + int((reading / 65535) * 5000)
    
    # Render X repeatedly
    # Display logic
    for col in font_x:
        show_col(col)
        utime.sleep_us(delay_us)
        
    show_col(0)
    utime.sleep_us(delay_us * 3) # Space
    
    # User feedback loop isn't printed every frame to avoid lag
    # But for calibration, maybe print occasionally?
    # print(f"Delay: {delay_us}") 
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Variable Swing**: User must try to swing at a consistent speed while calibrating.
*   **ADC Noise**: Jittery values might cause flickering width.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Auto-Cal**: Use accelerometer to measure swing speed and auto-set delay.

## 1. Project 1100: Advanced POV Clock Prototype

### 2. Learning Objective
"Holographic Clock". Displays current `HH:MM` in mid-air using Persistence of Vision. Updates every minute. Includes Real-Time Clock (RTC) integration.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Integrated System**: Combining POV, RTC, and String Formatting.
*   **Real-Time Clock**: Keeping accurate time.
*   **Dynamic Rendering**: Generating pixel data from changing strings.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **8 LEDs**
*   **RTC (DS3231 or internal)**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
*   **LED 0-7** -> GP0-GP7

### 6. Blocks Used
*   from **Smart IO**, drag **`rtc_get_time`**
*   From **Text**, drag **`create_text_with`**

### 7. Variables
*   **hh, mm, time_str**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide
1. **Initialize**:
    *   Setup POV Hardware.
    *   Setup RTC (Internal). Set default time if specific required.
    *   Define Font (Digits 0-9 and Colon).
2. **Main Loop**:
    *   **Get Time**: Read RTC.
    *   **Format**: Create string `HH:MM`.
    *   **Smart Display**:
        *   Iterate through string characters.
        *   Render columns with calibrated delay (e.g., 2000us).
        *   Repeat display loop for duration of swing/cycle.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Swing**: "12:00".
2.  **Wait**: Minute passes.
3.  **Swing**: "12:01".

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import utime

leds = [machine.Pin(i, machine.Pin.OUT) for i in range(8)]

# Font for Digits 0-9 and :
# (Simplified 3x5 or 4x7)
font = {
    '0': [0x1F, 0x11, 0x1F],
    '1': [0x00, 0x1F, 0x00],
    '2': [0x1D, 0x15, 0x17],
    '3': [0x15, 0x15, 0x1F],
    '4': [0x07, 0x04, 0x1F],
    '5': [0x17, 0x15, 0x0D],
    '6': [0x1F, 0x15, 0x0D],
    '7': [0x01, 0x01, 0x1F],
    '8': [0x1F, 0x15, 0x1F],
    '9': [0x07, 0x05, 0x1F],
    ':': [0x0A, 0x00], # Short Colon
    ' ': [0x00]
}

def show_col(v):
    for i in range(8): leds[i].value((v>>i)&1)

rtc = machine.RTC()
# rtc.datetime((2024, 1, 1, 0, 12, 0, 0, 0)) # Set time manually if needed

print("POV Clock Active")

DELAY_US = 2000

while True:
    t = rtc.datetime()
    # Format HH:MM
    # {:02d} not avail in micro python minimal sometimes, use manual
    hh = t[4]
    mm = t[5]
    
    h_str = str(hh) if hh > 9 else "0"+str(hh)
    m_str = str(mm) if mm > 9 else "0"+str(mm)
    
    time_str = f"{h_str}:{m_str}"
    
    # Render one frame
    for char in time_str:
        cols = font.get(char, font[' '])
        for c in cols:
            show_col(c)
            utime.sleep_us(DELAY_US)
        show_col(0)
        utime.sleep_us(DELAY_US)
    
    show_col(0)
    utime.sleep_ms(15) # Inter-frame gap
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **RTC Drift**: Internal RTC drifts. Use NTP (Pico W) or DS3231 for long term.
*   **Reverse**: If swinging back, text mirrors. Use P1093 logic to fix.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Date Display**: Show Date on the back-swing!
